==============================================================================
3. REPOSITORY STRUCTURE ON DISK
==============================================================================

A BareSnap repository has this structure:
    repo/
    +-----config        Configuration: UUID, chunking, compression, encryption, KVV
    +-----snapshots/    Binary manifests (.snap)
    +-----packs/        Chunk containers (.pack)
    +-----index/        Index segments (.idx) + Bloom Filters (.blm)
    +-----cache         Persistent file cache for incrementals
    +-----tmp/          Temporaries (power-outage anti-cleaning)

Each snapshot (.snap) lists all entries (path, metadata,
chunk IDs) of a specific backup. Chunks are stored in packs.

The index maps each chunk ID to its location within a pack
(pack_id, offset, comp_size, uncomp_size, flags).

Bloom Filters (.blm) are 128 KB binary files that
accompany each .idx and allow querying chunk existence
without loading the full index into RAM.

LOCAL SSH CACHE (v2.1.4):

    ~/.cache/baresnap/
    +-----<repo_hash_1>/
    |     +-----index/
    |           1787768297791881676.idx
    |           1787768639656186268.idx
    |           ...
    +-----<repo_hash_2>/
    |     +-----index/
    ...

.idx segments are cached locally to avoid re-downloading them
on each incremental backup over SSH.

==============================================================================
4. BINARY FORMAT
==============================================================================

Compatibility: FORMAT_VERSION=1. All little-endian. Magics
are 8 bytes without guaranteed NUL: compare with memcmp, never
with strlen or strcmp.

Magics:
    Config:           BSCFG001  (8 bytes)
    Pack header:      BSPK0001  (8 bytes)
    Pack footer:      BSPKFT01  (8 bytes)
    Index segment:    BSIX0001  (8 bytes)
    Plain snapshot:   BSSN0001  (8 bytes)
    File cache:       BSCACH01  (8 bytes)
    Encrypted snap:   BRSNAP2 followed by 0x01
    Bloom filter:     BRSBLM01  (8 bytes)

IMPORTANT: The encrypted snapshot magic contains the 0x01 byte.

Using strlen on it is prohibited. Always use memcmp with
length 8.

Flags:
    BRS_FLAG_ENCRYPTED         0x0001 (RepoConfig.flags)
    BRS_FLAG_HARDLINK          0x0002 (ManifestEntry.flags)
    BRS_FLAG_DELTA             0x0004 (ManifestEntry.flags)
    BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED  0x01   (ChunkLocation/PackEntry.flags)
    BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_ENCRYPTED   0x02   (ChunkLocation/PackEntry.flags)
    BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_ZSTD        0x04   (ChunkLocation/PackEntry.flags)

Chunk flag combinations:

- 0x00: uncompressed chunk, unencrypted
- 0x01: compressed with LZ4
- 0x05: compressed with ZSTD (COMPRESSED | ZSTD)
- 0x03: LZ4 compression + encrypted
- 0x07: ZSTD compression + encrypted

Enums:
    BrsFileType:     DIR=1, FILE=2, SYMLINK=3, HARDLINK=4, OTHER=5
    BrsCompression:  NONE=0, LZ4=1, ZSTD=2
    BrsHashAlgo:     FNV1A_128=0, XXH3_128=1, BLAKE2B_128=2

============================================================================
4.1 PACKS BINARY FORMAT (.PACK)
============================================================================

Each pack is an append-only container of encrypted/compressed chunks.

Real physical structure (all little-endian):

[1] PACK HEADER (36 bytes)

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD                  DESCRIPTION
------  ----    ---------------------  ------------------------------------
0       8       BRS_MAGIC_PACK         Magic "BSPK0001"
8       4       BRS_FORMAT_VERSION     Format version (1)
12      8       pack_id                Pack ID (uint64 LE)
20      16      reserved               Zero (16 bytes)

[2] CHUNK DATA

- Sequence of chunk payloads.
- Each chunk is written as a contiguous block.
- The offset of each chunk is stored in the footer.

[3] PACK FOOTER

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD
------  ----    ---------------------
0       8       BRS_MAGIC_PACK_FOOTER  Magic "BSPKFT01"
8       8       pack_id                Pack ID
16      4       entry_count            Number of entries
20      ...     entries                Entry table
...     8       crc32c_final           CRC32C of the complete pack

FOOTER ENTRY (exactly 33 bytes, no padding):

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD
------  ----    ---------------------
0       16      chunk_id
16      8       offset
24      4       comp_size
28      4       uncomp_size
32      1       flags

BRS_FOOTER_ENTRY_SIZE = 33

NOTES:

- comp_size and uncomp_size are uint32_t.
- The final CRC is written as uint64_t but the CRC32C value is 32 bits.
- The CRC is calculated over all bytes preceding the CRC itself.
- This format is identical in local and SSH.

v2.3.8 — Critical layout correction

Important:
    Locally the format was functional.
    The failure ONLY manifested in SSH when backups exceeded
    ~750 MB and packs larger than 100 MB were generated.

comp_size and uncomp_size fields of each footer entry are
written and read as uint32_t, not uint64_t.

BRS_FOOTER_ENTRY_SIZE changes from 41 bytes to 33 bytes:

    chunk_id(16) + offset(8) + comp_size(4) + uncomp_size(4) + flags(1)

Corrects:
    - cannot read pack footer
    - checksum mismatch
    - mismatches in multi-segment SSH backups

============================================================================
4.2 SNAPSHOTS BINARY FORMAT (.SNAP)
============================================================================

Each snapshot is a complete manifest of the backup at a point in time.
There are two variants depending on repository encryption.

HEADER (8 bytes, always cleartext):
+------------------------------------------------------------------+
| OFFSET 0-7: MAGIC                                                |
|   Plain:    "BSSN0001"  (8 bytes ASCII)                          |
|   Encrypted: "BRSNAP2\x01"  (7 bytes ASCII + 0x01)               |
|                                                                  |
|   IMPORTANT: The encrypted magic contains the 0x01 byte.         |
|   Using strlen() on it is prohibited. Use memcmp(x, magic, 8).   |
+------------------------------------------------------------------+

PLAIN MANIFEST BODY (little-endian):
+------------------------------------------------------------------+
| OFFSET  SIZE     FIELD              DESCRIPTION                  |
+------------------------------------------------------------------+
| 0       4        format_version     uint32 LE, value 1           |
| 4       16       snapshot_id        UUID v4 of this snapshot     |
| 20      16       repo_uuid          Repository UUID              |
| 36      16       parent_id          Parent snapshot UUID         |
|                                     (zeros if first)             |
| 52      8        created_ns         Creation timestamp (ns)      |
| 60      4+N      hostname           length-prefixed string       |
| var     4+N      root_path          length-prefixed string       |
| var     8        entry_count        Total entry count            |
| var     8        file_count         File count                   |
| var     8        dir_count          Directory count              |
| var     8        symlink_count      Symlink count                |
| var     8        logical_bytes      Total logical bytes          |
| var     8        chunk_refs         Chunk references             |
+------------------------------------------------------------------+

ENTRIES (repeated entry_count times):
+------------------------------------------------------------------+
| OFFSET  SIZE     FIELD              DESCRIPTION                  |
+------------------------------------------------------------------+
| 0       4+N      path               length-prefixed string       |
| var     1        type               BrsFileType (1-5)            |
| var     2        flags              LE uint16                    |
|                                     0x02 = HARDLINK              |
|                                     0x04 = DELTA                 |
| var     4        mode               POSIX permissions (0644, etc.)|
| var     4        uid                User ID                      |
| var     4        gid                Group ID                     |
| var     8        size               File size (bytes)            |
| var     8        mtime_ns           mtime in nanoseconds         |
| var     8        ctime_ns           ctime in nanoseconds         |
|                                                                  |
| -- Only if type == SYMLINK --                                    |
| var     4+N      symlink_target     length-prefixed string       |
|                                                                  |
| -- Only if type == FILE and flag HARDLINK --                     |
| var     4+N      hardlink_to        length-prefixed string       |
|                                                                  |
| -- Only if type == FILE --                                       |
| var     4        delta_source_count Only if DELTA flag           |
| var     16*N     delta_source[]     Only if DELTA flag           |
| var     4        chunk_count        File chunk count             |
| var     16*N     chunks[]           Array of BrsChunkId          |
+------------------------------------------------------------------+

IMPORTANT:
- `dev` and `ino` are NOT part of the .snap format.
- For directories nothing is written after ctime_ns.
- For symlinks and hardlinks no chunks are written.

PLAIN FOOTER:
+------------------------------------------------------------------+
| OFFSET  SIZE     FIELD              DESCRIPTION                  |
+------------------------------------------------------------------+
| var     8        checksum           FNV1A-64 of entire body      |
|                                     preceding the footer         |
+------------------------------------------------------------------+

COMPLETE DIAGRAM OF A PLAIN .snap:
+==========================================+
|  MAGIC: "BSSN0001"                 8 B   |
+==========================================+
|  format_version (u32 LE)           4 B   |
|  snapshot_id (UUID)               16 B   |
|  repo_uuid (UUID)                 16 B   |
|  parent_id (UUID)                 16 B   |
|  created_ns (u64 LE)               8 B   |
|  hostname (len + bytes)           var    |
|  root_path (len + bytes)          var    |
|  entry_count (u64 LE)              8 B   |
|  file_count (u64 LE)               8 B   |
|  dir_count (u64 LE)                8 B   |
|  symlink_count (u64 LE)            8 B   |
|  logical_bytes (u64 LE)            8 B   |
|  chunk_refs (u64 LE)               8 B   |
+==========================================+
|  ENTRY 0                                 |
|    path (len + bytes)                    |
|    type | flags | mode | uid | gid       |
|    size | mtime | ctime                  |
|    optional symlink/hardlink             |
|    optional delta_source                 |
|    chunk_count + chunks[16B × N]         |
+------------------------------------------+
|  ...                                     |
+==========================================+
|  FNV1A-64 checksum (u64 LE)        8 B   |
+==========================================+

COMPLETE DIAGRAM OF AN ENCRYPTED .snap:
+==========================================+
|  MAGIC: "BRSNAP2\x01"              8 B   |
+==========================================+
|                                         |
|  COMPLETE ENCRYPTION OF:                |
|    entire plain snapshot                |
|    including its header, entries        |
|    and FNV1A-64 checksum                |
|                                         |
|  Algorithms:                            |
|    XChaCha20-Poly1305                   |
|    or AES-256-GCM                       |
|                                         |
|  No additional clear footer.            |
+==========================================+

NOTES:

- The snapshot is written AT THE END of the create pipeline, after
  all packs and the index. This guarantees a power outage
  never leaves a snapshot pointing to incomplete data.

- The FNV1A-64 checksum covers the entire plain body preceding the footer.
  A single corrupted byte causes verify to fail.

- In encrypted repositories, that checksum travels inside the encryption.
  Outer integrity relies on the encryption algorithm tag.


============================================================================
4.3 INDEX SEGMENTS BINARY FORMAT (.IDX)
============================================================================

An index segment contains the global chunk index.

Each .idx segment is stored in:

    <repo>/index/<segment_id>.idx

The segment_id is a uint64_t.

All fields are little-endian.

------------------------------------------------------------
INDEX SEGMENT HEADER (44 bytes)
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
0       8       BRS_MAGIC_INDEX    Magic "BSIX0001"
8       4       format_version     uint32 LE, value 1
12      8       segment_id         uint64 LE
20      16      reserved           Zero (16 bytes)
36      8       entry_count        uint64 LE, entry count

Total header: 44 bytes.

------------------------------------------------------------
INDEX ENTRY (exactly 41 bytes)
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
0       16      chunk_id           Chunk hash (16 bytes)
16      8       pack_id            ID of the pack containing the chunk
24      8       offset             Chunk offset within the pack
32      4       comp_size          Compressed/encrypted size
36      4       uncomp_size        Original size
40      1       flags              Chunk flags

Total entry: 41 bytes.

IMPORTANT:
    Do not confuse with .pack footer entry:

        .idx  -> 41 bytes per entry
        .pack -> 33 bytes per entry

------------------------------------------------------------
FOOTER / CHECKSUM
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
var     8       checksum           FNV1A-64 (uint64 LE)

The FNV1A-64 checksum is calculated over all bytes preceding
the checksum itself.

The reader does:

- stored_csum = last 8 bytes
- computed     = brs_fnv1a_64(data, size - 8)

If stored_csum == 0:
    checksum is not validated.

If stored_csum != 0 and does not match:
    warning: index segment %llu: checksum mismatch, skipping

------------------------------------------------------------
COMPLETE DIAGRAM
------------------------------------------------------------

+--------------------------------------+
| "BSIX0001"                      8 B  |
| format_version (u32 LE)         4 B  |
| segment_id (u64 LE)             8 B  |
| reserved (16 bytes)            16 B  |
| entry_count (u64 LE)            8 B  |
+--------------------------------------+
| ENTRY 0                              |
|   chunk_id (16 B)                    |
|   pack_id (8 B)                      |
|   offset (8 B)                       |
|   comp_size (4 B)                    |
|   uncomp_size (4 B)                  |
|   flags (1 B)                        |
+--------------------------------------+
| ...                                  |
+--------------------------------------+
| FNV1A-64 checksum (u64 LE)      8 B  |
+--------------------------------------+

------------------------------------------------------------
v2.3.8 NOTES
------------------------------------------------------------

- Locally the index was already working fine.

- The failure only appeared in SSH when backups exceeded ~750 MB
  and multiple packs were generated per rotation.

- Previously, lateral per-pack indices were written during rotation,
  which could generate checksum mismatch.

- Now a SINGLE global .idx segment is written with all chunks
  from all packs.

- Entries of rotated packs are accumulated in the global BrsIndexMap
  before writing the final index.

============================================================================
4.4 BLOOM FILTERS BINARY FORMAT (.BLM)
============================================================================

A bloom filter is saved in:

    <repo>/index/<segment_id>.blm

segment_id matches the .idx segment it accompanies.

All fields are little-endian.

------------------------------------------------------------
BLOOM FILTER HEADER (fixed 24 bytes)
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
0       8       magic              "BRSBLM01"
8       8       size_bits          uint64 LE, filter bit count
16      1       num_hashes         uint8, hash function count
17      7       reserved           Zero (7 bytes)

Total header: 24 bytes.

------------------------------------------------------------
BLOOM FILTER DATA
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
24      N       bits               Byte array

Where:

    N = ceil(size_bits / 8)

Each filter bit is stored as a real bit inside that array.

------------------------------------------------------------
TOTAL SIZE
------------------------------------------------------------

    total = 24 + ceil(size_bits / 8)

------------------------------------------------------------
COMPLETE DIAGRAM
------------------------------------------------------------

+--------------------------------------+
| "BRSBLM01"                      8 B  |
| size_bits (uint64 LE)           8 B  |
| num_hashes (uint8)              1 B  |
| reserved (7 bytes)              7 B  |
+--------------------------------------+
| bits[ceil(size_bits/8)]         N B  |
+--------------------------------------+

------------------------------------------------------------
IMPORTANT NOTES
------------------------------------------------------------

- The .blm has NO checksum.

- The reader requires a minimum of 24 bytes and that the magic be
  exactly "BRSBLM01".

- If the file is shorter than 24 + bytes, it is considered corrupted
  and not loaded.

- In SSH mode, .blm files are uploaded with the same reliable
  mechanism as packs and indices (upload_pack), not via VFS write-back.

- Each .idx segment normally has an associated .blm with the
  same segment_id.

- The bloom filter is only an auxiliary acceleration structure.
  If missing, the engine can rebuild it or use the full index.


============================================================================
4.5 CONFIGURATION BINARY FORMAT (CONFIG)
============================================================================

The configuration file is located at:

    <repo>/config

All fields are little-endian.

------------------------------------------------------------
BASE STRUCTURE
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
0       8       magic              "BSCFG001"
8       4       format_version     uint32 LE, value 1
12      16      uuid               Repository UUID
28      8       created_ns         uint64 LE, creation date
36      4       chunk_min          uint32 LE
40      4       chunk_avg          uint32 LE
44      4       chunk_max          uint32 LE
48      1       hash_algo          uint8 (0=FNV1A,1=XXH3,2=BLAKE2B)
49      1       compression        uint8 (0=NONE,1=LZ4,2=ZSTD)
50      4       flags              uint32 LE
54      1       encrypted          uint8

    encrypted:
        0 = unencrypted
        1 = ChaCha20-Poly1305
        2 = AES-256-GCM

------------------------------------------------------------
ENCRYPTION SECTION (only if encrypted != 0)
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
55      16      crypto_salt        Random salt for KDF
71      4       kdf_nb_blocks      Argon2 parameter
75      4       kdf_nb_passes      Argon2 pass count
79      72      kvv                Key Verification Value

------------------------------------------------------------
ZSTD LEVEL FIELD (v1.1+)
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
var     1       zstd_level         ZSTD compression level

- Default value: 3
- Older repositories do not include this field.
- The parser detects it by checking if more than 8 bytes remain
  before the checksum.

------------------------------------------------------------
CHECKSUM
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
var     8       checksum           FNV1A-64 (uint64 LE)

    The checksum is calculated over all preceding bytes.

------------------------------------------------------------
COMPLETE DIAGRAM
------------------------------------------------------------

+--------------------------------------+
| "BSCFG001"                      8 B  |
| format_version (u32 LE)         4 B  |
| uuid (16 bytes)                16 B  |
| created_ns (u64 LE)             8 B  |
| chunk_min (u32 LE)              4 B  |
| chunk_avg (u32 LE)              4 B  |
| chunk_max (u32 LE)              4 B  |
| hash_algo (u8)                  1 B  |
| compression (u8)                1 B  |
| flags (u32 LE)                  4 B  |
| encrypted (u8)                  1 B  |
+--------------------------------------+
| ONLY IF encrypted != 0:              |
|   crypto_salt (16 bytes)             |
|   kdf_nb_blocks (u32 LE)             |
|   kdf_nb_passes (u32 LE)             |
|   kvv (72 bytes)                     |
+--------------------------------------+
| zstd_level (u8)                  1 B  |
+--------------------------------------+
| FNV1A-64 checksum (u64 LE)      8 B  |
+--------------------------------------+

------------------------------------------------------------
IMPORTANT NOTES
------------------------------------------------------------

- The `encrypted` field is a uint8, not a 0/1 boolean.
  A value of 2 indicates AES-256-GCM.

- The reader is compatible with old configurations that lack
  zstd_level: in that case it defaults to 3.

- If the checksum is 0, it is not validated.
  If it is non-zero and does not match, the configuration is rejected.

- The KVV (Key Verification Value) allows validating the passphrase
  before attempting to decrypt data.

============================================================================
4.6 FILE CACHE BINARY FORMAT (CACHE)
============================================================================

The file cache is saved at:

    <repo>/cache

All fields are little-endian.

------------------------------------------------------------
FILE CACHE HEADER
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
0       8       magic              "BSCACH01"
8       4       format_version     uint32 LE, value 1
12      8       entry_count        uint64 LE, entry count

Total header: 20 bytes.

------------------------------------------------------------
CACHE ENTRY
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
0       4+N      path              file path (length-prefixed)
var     8        dev               Device ID
var     8        ino               Inode number
var     8        size              File size
var     8        mtime_ns          mtime in nanoseconds
var     8        ctime_ns          ctime in nanoseconds
var     4        mode              POSIX permissions
var     4        chunk_count       File chunk count
var     16*N     chunks[]          Array of BrsChunkId

path and chunks fields are of variable length.

path is stored as:

    uint32 path_len
    bytes  path[path_len]

without a terminating NUL character.

------------------------------------------------------------
CHECKSUM
------------------------------------------------------------

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD              DESCRIPTION
------  ----    -----------------  -------------------------
var     8        checksum           FNV1A-64 (uint64 LE)

    The checksum is calculated over all preceding bytes.

------------------------------------------------------------
COMPLETE DIAGRAM
------------------------------------------------------------

+--------------------------------------+
| "BSCACH01"                      8 B  |
| format_version (u32 LE)         4 B  |
| entry_count (u64 LE)            8 B  |
+--------------------------------------+
| ENTRY 0                              |
|   path_len (u32 LE)                  |
|   path (path_len bytes)              |
|   dev (u64 LE)                       |
|   ino (u64 LE)                       |
|   size (u64 LE)                      |
|   mtime_ns (u64 LE)                  |
|   ctime_ns (u64 LE)                  |
|   mode (u32 LE)                      |
|   chunk_count (u32 LE)               |
|   chunks[chunk_count × 16 bytes]     |
+--------------------------------------+
| ...                                  |
+--------------------------------------+
| FNV1A-64 checksum (u64 LE)      8 B  |
+--------------------------------------+

------------------------------------------------------------
IMPORTANT NOTES
------------------------------------------------------------

- The file cache is an auxiliary acceleration structure.
  If missing or corrupted, the engine regenerates it from scratch.

- If checksum does not match, content is discarded and cache
  is restarted as empty and usable.

- If `stored_checksum == 0`, it is not validated.

- Format is identical to original C++ `cache.cpp`.

- Cache contains no chunk data, only references to
  chunks already existing in packs.

============================================================================
5. DELTA ENCODING WITH XDELTA3
============================================================================

BareSnap can save, for files that change little between
snapshots, a binary delta generated with xdelta3.

This reduces space occupied in packs, especially in text files,
small binaries, and successive versions of the same file.

------------------------------------------------------------
CREATION FLOW
------------------------------------------------------------

1. Locate previous version of the file in file cache
   or existing index.

2. Reconstruct previous content:

       old_content

3. Read new content:

       new_content

4. Calculate delta:

       delta_payload = xd3_encode_memory(new, old)

5. If delta meets savings condition, it is used:

       delta_len * BRS_DELTA_MIN_RATIO < new_size

   Otherwise, delta is discarded and file is stored
   as normal chunks.

------------------------------------------------------------
ACTIVATION CRITERIA
------------------------------------------------------------

Delta is only attempted if:

    e->size > 0
    e->size <= delta_max

Where:

    delta_max = BRS_DELTA_MAX_SIZE
        for normal mode.

    delta_max = BRS_DELTA_MAX_SIZE_BINARY
        when using --delta-binary.

Current values:

    BRS_DELTA_MAX_SIZE         = 256 KB
    BRS_DELTA_MAX_SIZE_BINARY  = 256 MB
    BRS_DELTA_MIN_RATIO        = 2

This means:

- A delta is only stored if it takes up less than half
  of the new file.

- By default, large files (>256 KB) do not use delta,
  unless --delta-binary is enabled.

------------------------------------------------------------
DELTA IN SSH
------------------------------------------------------------

By default, delta encoding is DISABLED in SSH repositories.

Reason:

    The cost of reconstructing previous versions can be high
    and avoids overloading the connection.

To enable it explicitly:

    BRS_SSH_DELTA=1

Example:

    BRS_SSH_DELTA=1 ./baresnap create ssh://... /path

------------------------------------------------------------
STORAGE IN SNAPSHOT
------------------------------------------------------------

When a file uses delta, the manifest entry contains:

    delta_source_count
    delta_source_chunks[]
    chunk_count
    chunks[]

Where:

- delta_source_chunks[] are chunks of the previous version
  serving as the delta base.

- chunks[] is the single chunk containing the encoded delta.

The delta itself is treated as a normal chunk:

- compressed (LZ4/ZSTD)
- encrypted if repo is encrypted
- stored in a pack

Therefore, the delta is not written as raw visible VCDIFF,
but as another chunk in the pipeline.

------------------------------------------------------------
RESTORATION
------------------------------------------------------------

To restore a file with delta:

1. Reconstruct base content:

       old_content
       from delta_source_chunks[]

2. Reconstruct delta payload:

       delta_payload
       from chunks[]

3. Apply:

       xd3_decode_memory(old_content, delta_payload)

4. Verify resulting size matches:

       x->size

If size does not match or xdelta3 returns error:

    ERROR: delta restore failed for <path>

And the file is not written as valid.

------------------------------------------------------------
DELTA PIPELINE
------------------------------------------------------------

Creation:

    Read → Delta encoding → Compression → Encryption → Pack

Restoration:

    Pack → Decryption → Decompression → Delta decoding → Write

Delta is applied BEFORE compression, because compression
would destroy the patterns xdelta3 needs to function.

------------------------------------------------------------
IMPORTANT NOTES
------------------------------------------------------------

- The BRS_FLAG_DELTA flag is stored in manifest entry flags.

- Delta source is referenced with already existing chunks, so
  prune must preserve source chunks as long as a snapshot
  needs them.

- If the pack containing source chunks is deleted, delta restore
  will fail and verify will detect it.

- In real ELF/PE binaries, --delta-binary can save a
significant amount of space between versions.

============================================================================
6. BLOOM FILTER AND INDEX LAZY LOADING
============================================================================

The global index can be very large in backups with many
chunks[cite: 3]. To avoid reading all .idx segments in each
operation, BareSnap uses Bloom filters as an accelerator[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT IS A BLOOM FILTER?
------------------------------------------------------------

A Bloom filter is a probabilistic structure that allows
knowing whether a chunk can be in an index segment without
needing to read the complete index[cite: 3].

It can answer:

    "definitely NOT present"
    "might be present"

It never answers:

    "definitely present"

Therefore, upon a positive match, the real index is queried to
confirm[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
CURRENT PARAMETERS
------------------------------------------------------------

- BRS_BLOOM_BITS_PER_SEG = 16 M bits = 2 MB per segment[cite: 3].
- BRS_BLOOM_NUM_HASHES   = 5[cite: 3]

With these values, the false positive rate is
negligible even with hundreds of thousands of chunks[cite: 3].

Theoretical rate:

    p = (1 - e^(-k*n/m))^k[cite: 3]

Example:

    k=5[cite: 3]
    m=16777216[cite: 3]
    n=1000000[cite: 3]

    p ≈ 1.5e-12[cite: 3]

Therefore, in practice, unnecessary .idx segments are not
loaded[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
LOADING FLOW
------------------------------------------------------------

### Local mode and SSH without full index

1. All available .blm files are loaded[cite: 3]:

       brs_load_bloom_set()[cite: 3]

2. For each chunk to be searched[cite: 3]:

   - The segment's Bloom filter is checked[cite: 3].
   - If the Bloom filter says it can NOT be present, the segment is ignored[cite: 3].
   - If it says it MIGHT be present, the segment's .idx is loaded[cite: 3]:

       brs_load_index_segment()[cite: 3]

   - The real index is queried[cite: 3]:

       brs_index_map_get()[cite: 3]

3. The loaded segment is kept in memory for subsequent
   queries[cite: 3].

This is called lazy loading[cite: 3]:

    The entire index is not loaded at the beginning[cite: 3].
    Only segments are loaded when necessary[cite: 3].

### SSH mode with full index

If the entire remote index can be loaded into RAM[cite: 3]:

    g_brs_ssh_full_index = 1[cite: 3]

In that case, neither Bloom filters nor lazy loading are used[cite: 3]. The complete index is used
directly[cite: 3].

If the complete remote index cannot be loaded[cite: 3]:

    warning: remote index references missing packs[cite: 3] (Note: matching source text warning phrasing context)

And it switches to the flow with Bloom filters[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
PER-SEGMENT BLOOM FILTER
------------------------------------------------------------

Each .idx segment has an associated .blm file[cite: 3]:

    <repo>/index/<segment_id>.idx[cite: 3]
    <repo>/index/<segment_id>.blm[cite: 3]

The Bloom filter is built over the segment's chunk_ids[cite: 3].

Insertion function[cite: 3]:

    brs_bloom_add()[cite: 3]

Query function[cite: 3]:

    brs_bloom_check()[cite: 3]

------------------------------------------------------------
.blm FORMAT
------------------------------------------------------------

See section 4.4[cite: 3].

Summary[cite: 3]:

    "BRSBLM01"              8 bytes[cite: 3]
    size_bits               8 bytes[cite: 3]
    num_hashes              1 byte[cite: 3]
    reserved                7 bytes[cite: 3]
    bits[]                  ceil(size_bits/8) bytes[cite: 3]

The .blm file does not carry a checksum[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
IMPORTANT NOTES
------------------------------------------------------------

- A Bloom filter false positive NEVER produces corrupted data[cite: 3].
  It only implies loading an index that might not contain the chunk[cite: 3].

- The final check is always performed against the real index[cite: 3].

- If .blm files are missing, the engine can directly load the
  .idx files or regenerate the Bloom filters[cite: 3].

- In SSH, .blm files are uploaded using the same reliable mechanism as
  packs and indexes[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
CHANGES IN v2.3.8
------------------------------------------------------------

- Verification of packs referenced by the existing index was added
  before using cache or Bloom filters[cite: 3].

- If the remote index references a pack that does not exist[cite: 3]:

      error: remote index references missing packs[cite: 3]

  And the create operation is aborted[cite: 3].

- This prevents cache and Bloom filters from masking pack
  loss[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
PERFORMANCE
------------------------------------------------------------

For large repositories, lazy loading reduces[cite: 3]:

- RAM consumption[cite: 3]
- number of .idx reads[cite: 3]
- start time for create/restore/verify[cite: 3]

The 2 MB Bloom filters per segment take up little space compared to the
total index size in repositories with millions of chunks[cite: 3].

==============================================================================
7. ZSTD COMPRESSION AND MIXED CODECS (PER-CHUNK DECISION)
==============================================================================

Since v2.3.0, BareSnap supports multi-level and multi-algorithm dynamic compression[cite: 3].

Base compression is defined in 'init' and recorded in the repo config, but the
'create' pipeline can be intercepted on-the-fly via session variables[cite: 3]:
    $ BRS_CREATE_COMPRESSION=zstd BRS_CREATE_ZSTD_LEVEL=15 ./baresnap create ...[cite: 3]

Flow and Binary Coexistence (Atomic Logic)[cite: 3]:

- The codec is decided at the INDIVIDUAL CHUNK level, not at the repository or snapshot level[cite: 3].
- LZ4 chunks (bit BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED active) and ZSTD chunks (bits
  BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED and BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_ZSTD active) coexist in the same
  physical '.pack' tables and the same '.idx' index segments without friction[cite: 3].

- The 'restore', 'verify', and 'extract' operations read the bitmask of each
  block in the hot path[cite: 3]. The decoder invokes the correct decompressor on-the-fly,
  making the engine immune to algorithm mixing[cite: 3].

The Entropy Firewall (Early Abort)[cite: 3]:
It doesn't matter if you force ZSTD-22 or LZ4; before invoking heavy compressors, the
Worker executes the inline function 'brs_is_chunk_incompressible()'[cite: 3].
It samples exactly 1024 bytes at fixed skips on the thread stack (zero locks)[cite: 3].
If the unique byte histogram determines white noise (videos, .7z, zip, jpg),
the engine turns off the BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED bit in RAM and sends the encrypted chunk
straight to disk[cite: 3].

ZSTD Levels and Memory Pressure on Dual Core[cite: 3]:

- Levels 1-12: Optimal for Core 2 Duo[cite: 3]. Burst speed in text logs[cite: 3].
- Levels 20-22 (Ultra): Consume >256 MB of RAM per thread[cite: 3]. Saturate CPU to 100%[cite: 3].
  Recommended only on modern hardware or leisurely nighttime tasks[cite: 3].


------------------------------------------------------------
CHUNK FLAGS
------------------------------------------------------------

Each chunk carries a flag bitmask in its index entry and in the
pack footer[cite: 3].

Actual flags[cite: 3]:

    BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED = 0x01[cite: 3]
    BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_ZSTD       = 0x04[cite: 3]

Interpretation[cite: 3]:

- Without `COMPRESSED`[cite: 3]:
    The chunk is saved without compression[cite: 3].

- With `COMPRESSED` and without `ZSTD`[cite: 3]:
    The chunk is compressed with LZ4[cite: 3].

- With `COMPRESSED` and with `ZSTD`[cite: 3]:
    The chunk is compressed with ZSTD[cite: 3].

That is[cite: 3]:

    LZ4  -> 0x01[cite: 3]
    ZSTD -> 0x01 | 0x04[cite: 3]

------------------------------------------------------------
PER-CHUNK DECISION
------------------------------------------------------------

The codec is decided at the INDIVIDUAL CHUNK level, not at the repository or
snapshot level[cite: 3].

Flow in create[cite: 3]:

1. Session compression is resolved[cite: 3]:

       brs_resolve_session_compression()[cite: 3]

   with config values and environment variables[cite: 3].

2. For each chunk[cite: 3]:

   a. It checks if it should be compressed[cite: 3]:

          use_compression
          && item.size > 0
          && !brs_is_chunk_incompressible(cdata, item.size)[cite: 3]

   b. If the session codec is ZSTD[cite: 3]:

          flags |= BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED | BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_ZSTD[cite: 3]

   c. If it is LZ4[cite: 3]:

          flags |= BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED[cite: 3]

   d. If the chunk is incompressible[cite: 3]:

          BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED is not added[cite: 3]
          the chunk is saved encrypted or raw, without compression[cite: 3]

Additionally, compression is only kept if it actually reduces the size[cite: 3]:

- ZSTD[cite: 3]:

      comp_len > 0 && comp_len < item.size[cite: 3]

- LZ4[cite: 3]:

      comp_len > 0 && comp_len < item.size[cite: 3]

If the result is not smaller, the original uncompressed chunk is saved[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
ENTROPY FIREWALL
------------------------------------------------------------

Before invoking a heavy compressor, the engine queries[cite: 3]:

    brs_is_chunk_incompressible(data, size)[cite: 3]

This function samples exactly 1024 bytes at fixed skips and builds
a stack histogram[cite: 3].

Actual criteria[cite: 3]:

- If there are more than 200 distinct symbols in the sample[cite: 3]:
    the chunk is considered incompressible[cite: 3].

- If there are at least 192 distinct symbols and the distribution is very flat[cite: 3]:
    the chunk is considered incompressible[cite: 3].

With 1024 samples and 256 buckets, the expected count per bucket is 4[cite: 3].
A squared deviation <= 2048 indicates white noise or already compressed
data[cite: 3].

When the firewall activates[cite: 3]:

- compression is skipped[cite: 3]
- the chunk is kept without `BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED`[cite: 3]
- wasting CPU on LZ4/ZSTD is avoided[cite: 3]

This typically occurs with videos, .7z, .zip, .jpg, and random data[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
LZ4 + ZSTD COEXISTENCE IN THE SAME REPO
------------------------------------------------------------

LZ4 chunks and ZSTD chunks can coexist in the same repo, in the
same packs, and in the same .idx segments[cite: 3].

When `restore`, `verify`, or `extract` read a chunk[cite: 3]:

    if (flags & BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED) {
        if (flags & BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_ZSTD)
            descomprimir_zstd();
        else
            descomprimir_lz4();
    }[cite: 3]

Therefore, the engine is immune to algorithm mixing[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
ZSTD LEVELS
------------------------------------------------------------

Levels 1-12[cite: 3]:

    Recommended for modest CPUs, like the Core 2 Duo T7500[cite: 3].
    Good balance between speed and ratio[cite: 3].

Levels 20-22[cite: 3]:

    Ultra mode[cite: 3].
    Consume high memory and CPU[cite: 3].
    Recommended only on modern hardware or nighttime tasks[cite: 3].

Since v2.3.5, the RAM guardian can automatically degrade the level
if available memory is scarce[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES
------------------------------------------------------------

    BRS_CREATE_COMPRESSION[cite: 3]
        none | lz4 | zstd[cite: 3]

    BRS_CREATE_ZSTD_LEVEL[cite: 3]
        1..22[cite: 3]

Example[cite: 3]:

    BRS_CREATE_COMPRESSION=zstd \
    BRS_CREATE_ZSTD_LEVEL=15 \
    ./baresnap create /repo /datos[cite: 3]

------------------------------------------------------------
SSH NOTE
------------------------------------------------------------

In SSH repositories, per-chunk compression remains active[cite: 3].

What is disabled by default is delta encoding[cite: 3]:

    BRS_SSH_DELTA=1[cite: 3]

to explicitly enable it[cite: 3].

Compression and delta are independent mechanisms[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
FLAGS SUMMARY
------------------------------------------------------------

    No compression:  flags = 0[cite: 3]
    LZ4:             flags = 0x01[cite: 3]
    ZSTD:            flags = 0x01 | 0x04[cite: 3]

==============================================================================
8. CLI - COMMAND REFERENCE
==============================================================================

General syntax[cite: 3]:
    baresnap <command> [options...][cite: 3]

Repository commands[cite: 3]:
    init <repo> [--encrypt] [--compression lz4|zstd] [--zstd-level N][cite: 3]
        Creates a new repository with unique UUID and timestamp[cite: 3].
        --encrypt: activates XChaCha20-Poly1305 encryption with Argon2[cite: 3].
        --compression: compression codec (lz4 by default, zstd optional)[cite: 3].
        --zstd-level: ZSTD level 1-22 (only with --compression zstd)[cite: 3].

    info <repo>[cite: 3]
        Displays repository statistics: UUID, chunking, codec,
        snapshots, packs, chunks, storage, index, cache,
        and delta encoding metrics[cite: 3]. In SSH remote repos, pack sizes
        are read via VFS (not local stat)[cite: 3].

    list <repo>[cite: 3]
        Lists all snapshots in the repository[cite: 3].

    verify <repo>[cite: 3]
        Verifies complete integrity: CRC32C of each pack,
        decryption and decompression (LZ4/ZSTD) of each chunk, hash of
        each chunk compared to its ID, chunks of each snapshot
        present in index and packs, delta_source_chunks present[cite: 3].

Data commands[cite: 3]:
    create <repo> <src>[,src2,...] [label] [--compression lz4|zstd][cite: 3]
           [--zstd-level N] [--delta-binary][cite: 3]
        Creates an incremental snapshot[cite: 3]. Accepts multiple sources
        separated by commas[cite: 3]. Optionally with a label[cite: 3].
        AUTO-INIT: if the repo does not exist, it initializes automatically
        with the indicated compression options[cite: 3].
        If config is missing but index metadata exists, create aborts
        with "inconsistent state" to avoid destroying an existing repo[cite: 3].
        If the repo already exists, --compression is ignored with a warning[cite: 3].

    restore <repo> <snap> <dest>[cite: 3]
        Restores a complete snapshot to a destination directory[cite: 3].
        Preserves permissions, uid/gid, timestamps, symlinks, and hardlinks[cite: 3].
        Automatically detects each chunk's codec (LZ4/ZSTD)[cite: 3].
        The snapshot name accepts partial prefixes: if the exact name
        does not exist, it searches by prefix in snapshots/[cite: 3].

    extract <repo> <snap> <dest> [paths...][cite: 3]
        Selective extraction[cite: 3]. Only extracts indicated paths relative
        to the original backup source[cite: 3]. Without paths, extracts the full snapshot[cite: 3].
        The engine automatically sanitizes paths with trailing or redundant slashes[cite: 3].

        Examples[cite: 3]:
        # Extract a specific folder from a remote SSH repository[cite: 3]
        baresnap extract ssh://test@192.168.1.5/backups 2026-08-27.snap ./out spartanpack[cite: 3]
        # Extract multiple specific files or folders[cite: 3]
        baresnap extract /mnt/repo snap.snap /tmp/out home/user/.bashrc docs/proyecto[cite: 3]

    ls <repo> <snap> [prefix] [-l] [-r] [-m text][cite: 3]
        Lists or searches inside a snapshot[cite: 3].

    diff <repo> <snapA> <snapB>[cite: 3]
        Shows differences between two snapshots[cite: 3].

    prune <repo> [options][cite: 3]
        Deletes old snapshots according to retention policy and
        rewrites packs to reclaim space (2 local concurrent workers)[cite: 3].
        Also cleans up orphaned .blm files[cite: 3].
        Over SSH, prune operates with 1 worker to maintain pipe stability[cite: 3].

Global options (v2.3.7)[cite: 3]:
  --version, -v      Show version and exit[cite: 3]
  --help, -h         Show this help[cite: 3]
  --timeout  <ms>    SSH timeout per operation (default: 60000)[cite: 3]
  --pass-fd  <N>     Read passphrase from file descriptor N[cite: 3]
                     (invisible in /proc/[PID]/cmdline and argv)[cite: 3]

Timeout usage examples[cite: 3]:
# Remote backup with 5-minute timeout (very slow satellite/WAN)[cite: 3]
baresnap create --timeout 300000 ssh://user@host:/repo ~/datos[cite: 3]

# 90-second timeout for LAN with slow remote disk[cite: 3]
baresnap --timeout 90000 create ssh://user@host:/repo ~/docs[cite: 3]

# Alternative form: flag before subcommand[cite: 3]
baresnap --timeout 60000 info ssh://user@host:/repo[cite: 3]

# --- Passphrase via fd (invisible in /proc/[PID]/cmdline and argv) ---
PASS="clave-secreta"[cite: 3]
baresnap --pass-fd 3 create /repo ~/docs 3<<<"$PASS"[cite: 3]
baresnap --pass-fd 3 create ssh://user@host:/repo ~/docs 3<<<"$PASS"[cite: 3]
unset PASS[cite: 3]

NOTE: --pass-fd prevents exposure in ps aux and /proc/[PID]/cmdline[cite: 3].
The fd is closed after reading and the buffer is cleared with secure_zero()
upon exit[cite: 3]. Absolute invisibility in /proc/[PID]/environ is not guaranteed
if the engine internally reuses environment channels for compatibility[cite: 3].

Remote commands (SSH)[cite: 3]:

    remote install <ssh_uri>[cite: 3]
        Deploys the `baresnap-remote` agent on the remote server[cite: 3].
        Creates necessary directories and copies the binary[cite: 3].
        Supports public key authentication AND interactive password[cite: 3].

    remote test <ssh_uri>[cite: 3]
        Verifies connectivity and response of the remote agent[cite: 3].

    Remote usage example with password[cite: 3]:
        baresnap remote install ssh://user@servidor:/ruta/al/repo[cite: 3]
        # Will prompt for password interactively[cite: 3].
        baresnap create ssh://user@servidor:/ruta/al/repo ~/docs[cite: 3]

    Remote usage example with public key[cite: 3]:
        ssh-copy-id user@servidor   # once[cite: 3]
        baresnap remote install ssh://user@servidor:/ruta/al/repo[cite: 3]
        baresnap create ssh://user@servidor:/ruta/al/repo ~/docs[cite: 3]

    Automation with password (tests, occasional)[cite: 3]:
        BARESNAP_SSH_PASSWORD="clave" baresnap create ssh://user@servidor:/ruta/al/repo ~/docs[cite: 3]

    tui [repo] [path][cite: 3]
        Launches the Midnight Commander-style ncurses TUI[cite: 3].

SSH environment variables (v2.1.4)[cite: 3]:
    BRS_SSH_DEBUG=1[cite: 3]
        Enables debug mode: displays each individual RPC on stderr[cite: 3].
    BRS_SSH_STRICT_FSYNC=1[cite: 3]
        Forces real fsync on each operation (lazy by default)[cite: 3].


NEW IN v2.3.8 CLI

The CLI is the same as in v2.3.7, but with these internal changes that
affect output and remote behavior[cite: 3]:

1. PACK PROGRESS

   During `create`, clean messages are displayed in addition to the bar[cite: 3]:

       [pack] Creating package 1...[cite: 3]
       [pack] Uploading package 1 (5688 chunks, 100.16 MB)[cite: 3]

   Previously only the `dedup` bar was visible, which cut off when rotating packs[cite: 3].

2. PACK UPLOAD BY REMOTE AGENT

   Packs no longer use external `scp` (this was only a test from an RC
   in 2.3.7) or VFS write-back[cite: 3].

   The existing SSH channel is used with[cite: 3]:

       BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK[cite: 3]
       BRS_MSG_DATA (4 MiB blocks)[cite: 3]
       Per-block ACK[cite: 3]
       Final size verification[cite: 3]

   This fixes corruption that only appeared in SSH with backups
   larger than ~750 MB[cite: 3].

3. EXISTING PACK VERIFICATION

   Before creating an incremental snapshot, `create` checks that all
   packs referenced by the existing index still exist[cite: 3].

   If a pack is missing[cite: 3]:

       error: pack file 'packs/...' does not exist[cite: 3]

   A false snapshot is no longer generated due to the cache[cite: 3].

4. SESSION OPTIONS UNCHANGED

   The following are maintained[cite: 3]:

       --timeout <ms>[cite: 3]
       --buffer-size <VAL>[cite: 3]
       --compression lz4|zstd[cite: 3]
       --zstd-level N[cite: 3]
       --delta-binary[cite: 3]
       --pass-fd <N>[cite: 3]

   No new flags have been added[cite: 3].

5. SSH BEHAVIOR

   - Delta encoding remains disabled by default in SSH[cite: 3].
   - It is enabled with[cite: 3]:

         BRS_SSH_DELTA=1[cite: 3]

   - Per-chunk compression remains active in SSH[cite: 3].
   - SSH timeout is controlled with[cite: 3]:

         --timeout <ms>[cite: 3]

     or with the obsolete variable BRS_SSH_TIMEOUT_MS if the old binary
     still supports it, but --timeout is recommended[cite: 3].

============================================================================
8.1 AUTO-INIT
============================================================================

When create detects that the repo does not exist (no config file),
it automatically initializes it before continuing[cite: 3]:
    $ baresnap create /mnt/nuevo/repo ~/docs --compression zstd[cite: 3]
    Repository not found at '/mnt/nuevo/repo'.[cite: 3]
    Initializing new repository...[cite: 3]
    initialized repo: /mnt/nuevo/repo (compression: zstd, level: 3)[cite: 3]
    Repository initialized successfully.[cite: 3]
    [continues with backup normally][cite: 3]

If the repo already exists and --compression is passed, a warning is emitted[cite: 3]:
    warning: repo already exists, --compression ignored[cite: 3]

============================================================================
8.2 ENCRYPTION AND PASSPHRASE AUTOMATION
============================================================================

The passphrase is requested interactively (without echo, via /dev/tty) or taken
from the BARESNAP_PASSPHRASE environment variable (maximum 1024
characters)[cite: 3].

IMPORTANT: Do not store the passphrase in plain text in cron scripts[cite: 3].

Use a volatile credentials mechanism (systemd-creds, vault, etc.)[cite: 3].

Typical cron entry (daily backup at 03:00) using systemd-creds[cite: 3]:
    0 3 * * * backup /usr/local/bin/baresnap-backup.sh[cite: 3]

The script loads the passphrase into memory[cite: 3]:
    export BARESNAP_PASSPHRASE="$(systemd-creds cat baresnap-repo-key)"[cite: 3]
    "$BARESNAP" create /mnt/backup/repo /home/usuario/docs >> /var/log/brs.log 2>&1[cite: 3]
    unset BARESNAP_PASSPHRASE[cite: 3]

============================================================================
8.3 SNAPSHOT RESOLUTION BY PREFIX
============================================================================

The restore/extract/ls/diff commands accept partial snapshot names[cite: 3].

If the exact name does not exist, resolve_snap_path() searches snapshots/ by prefix[cite: 3]:
    baresnap restore /repo mi-primer-backup_2026 /tmp/out[cite: 3]
    # Resolves to: mi-primer-backup_2026-08-23_21-07-01_12ec08ad.snap[cite: 3]

If there are multiple matches, a warning is emitted and the user is asked to use
'baresnap list' to get the full name[cite: 3].

============================================================================
8.4 HEALTH: ANOMALY VERIFICATION AND REPAIR
============================================================================

Usage[cite: 3]:
    baresnap health <repo> [--repair][cite: 3]
Verifies and repairs repository anomalies[cite: 3].

OPTIONS[cite: 3]:
    --repair      Repairs automatically without prompting[cite: 3].
                  Without --repair, only detects and displays anomalies[cite: 3].

DETECTED ANOMALIES[cite: 3]:

- Orphaned index segments (idx without referenced pack)[cite: 3]
- Packs without index segment (unindexed chunks)[cite: 3]
- Orphaned Bloom filters (blm without corresponding idx)[cite: 3]
- open_pack_id pointing to non-existent pack[cite: 3]
- Snapshot chunks not present in any index[cite: 3]
- Chunks pointing to non-existent packs[cite: 3]
- Corrupted snapshots (parse fail or checksum mismatch)[cite: 3]
- Missing or unreadable config (v2.2.2)[cite: 3]
- Zombie temporaries tmp/*.tmp (v2.2.2)[cite: 3]

REPAIR ACTIONS (--repair)[cite: 3]:
1. Purge zombie temporaries tmp/*.tmp[cite: 3]
2. Recreate config if missing or unreadable[cite: 3]
3. Delete orphaned index segments and their blooms[cite: 3]
4. Rebuild missing chunks from ALL packs[cite: 3]
5. Delete orphaned blooms[cite: 3]
6. Delete orphaned open_pack_id[cite: 3]
7. Re-verify snapshots after index rebuild[cite: 3]
8. Move corrupted snapshots to damaged/[cite: 3]

NEW IN v2.3.8[cite: 3]:

9. Verify that all packs referenced by indices exist[cite: 3].
   If a pack is missing, health detects it as an anomaly and create aborts[cite: 3].

EXAMPLES[cite: 3]:
    # Verify only (shows anomalies, does not repair)[cite: 3]
    baresnap health /mnt/backup/repo[cite: 3]
    # Verify and repair without prompting[cite: 3]
    baresnap health /mnt/backup/repo --repair[cite: 3]
    # In cron (no TTY, auto-repair)[cite: 3]
    baresnap health /mnt/backup/repo --repair </dev/null[cite: 3]

BEHAVIOR IN INCONSISTENT REPOS (v2.2.2)[cite: 3]:

If config is missing but index/ exists[cite: 3]:

- create ABORTS with "inconsistent state"[cite: 3]
- health detects the anomaly[cite: 3]
- health --repair recreates the config[cite: 3]
- create works again[cite: 3]

If both config AND index/ are missing[cite: 3]:

- create can auto-initialize (recoverable Frankenstein repo)[cite: 3]
- health --repair rebuilds index from packs[cite: 3]


NEW IN v2.3.8[cite: 3]:

- If index/ exists but any referenced pack has been
  deleted[cite: 3]:

      health detects the missing pack[cite: 3]
      create does NOT create a new snapshot[cite: 3]
      health --repair moves affected snapshots to damaged/[cite: 3]

  Previously, the cache could mask this issue and generate
  snapshots pointing to non-existent packs[cite: 3].

NOTE ON ENCRYPTED REPOS[cite: 3]:
If an encrypted repo's config is lost (salt, KVV), repair can restore the
structure but NOT the decryption key[cite: 3]. Encrypted data will be inaccessible without
the original passphrase[cite: 3].

FIX F1 — ENCRYPTION-AWARE CONFIG RECREATION (v2.3.5)[cite: 3]:
When health --repair recreates a missing or unreadable config, it now first scans
existing snapshots looking for the encrypted snapshot magic
(BRSNAP2\x01) via health_detect_encrypted_snapshots()[cite: 3]. If it detects at
least one encrypted snapshot, it recreates the config with encrypted=1 and emits a
warning, avoiding the recreation of a plain config over a repo whose content is
encrypted[cite: 3]. This prevents silent corruption of the "Encrypted Frankenstein
Repo": without this fix, subsequent operations would write and
interpret metadata under erroneous cryptographic assumptions[cite: 3].

Note: The original Argon2 salt/KVV lived in the lost config[cite: 3]. Repair
restores structural operability and the encryption flag, but data
still requires the original passphrase[cite: 3].
Associated test: section 107 of mega_test (11 tests)[cite: 3].

NEW IN v2.3.8[cite: 3]:

- Packs referenced by the existing index that no longer exist in
  packs/[cite: 3]

  This anomaly is detected BEFORE reusing the cache or creating an
  incremental snapshot[cite: 3].

  Example output[cite: 3]:

      error: pack file 'packs/2038047230406222881.pack' does not exist[cite: 3]

  Without this check, a backup with 100% cache hits could
  generate a fake snapshot even though packs had disappeared[cite: 3].

------------------------------------------------------------
v2.3.8 CHANGES IN HEALTH
------------------------------------------------------------

- health shares existing pack validation with create[cite: 3].
- If a pack is missing, health detects it in the verification phase[cite: 3].
- health --repair moves damaged snapshots to damaged/[cite: 3].
- Detection does not depend on cache; the real index is queried[cite: 3].
- In SSH, detection works the same as locally[cite: 3].

==============================================================================
9. GUI - YAD (SHELL)
==============================================================================

The GUI is a shell script (gui/baresnap-gui.sh) that uses yad
for dialogs[cite: 3]. The C11 engine is the same; the GUI merely wraps it[cite: 3].

Requirements[cite: 3]:

- yad installed ("yad" package on most distros)[cite: 3]

Functionality[cite: 3]:
Main menu with operations[cite: 3]:
1.  Initialize Repo: create repo with compression selection
    (LZ4/ZSTD + level), optional encryption[cite: 3].
2.  Create Backup: auto-initializes if necessary, with compression
    and encryption options[cite: 3].
3.  Restore: choose snapshot, destination, restore[cite: 3].
4.  Verify: complete integrity[cite: 3].
5.  List Snapshots[cite: 3].
6.  Explore Snapshot (text): list files[cite: 3].
7.  Interactive Explorer: browse snapshot content in GUI[cite: 3].
8.  Extract Files: selective extraction[cite: 3].
9.  Prune: retention policy with dry-run[cite: 3].
10. Info: repository statistics[cite: 3].
11. Diff: compare two snapshots[cite: 3].

Real-time progress (named pipe)[cite: 3]:

The engine detects it is running under the GUI via the
BARESNAP_PROGRESS_PIPE environment variable and writes integer percentages

(0-100) to the named pipe[cite: 3]. The script does[cite: 3]:
    mkfifo /tmp/brs_progress[cite: 3]
    yad --progress --percentage=0 < /tmp/brs_progress &[cite: 3]
    BARESNAP_PROGRESS_PIPE=/tmp/brs_progress ./build/baresnap create ...[cite: 3]

Result: live 0-100 real progress bar[cite: 3].

Connection to C engine[cite: 3]:
The script invokes the static build/baresnap binary directly[cite: 3].
The passphrase is passed via the BARESNAP_PASSPHRASE environment variable
within the same process and cleared with unset upon completion[cite: 3].

==============================================================================
10. TUI - NCURSES
==============================================================================

The TUI is integrated into the same CLI binary (baresnap tui)[cite: 3].
Midnight Commander style: dual twin panels, F-key bar
at the bottom, and drop-down menu with F9[cite: 3].

Usage[cite: 3]:
    ./build/baresnap tui                        # picker: choose repo[cite: 3]
    ./build/baresnap tui /mnt/backup/repo       # already opened repo[cite: 3]
    ./build/baresnap tui /mnt/backup/repo ~/    # with initial local path[cite: 3]

Right panel modes[cite: 3]:

- PM_PICKER:        browsing disk looking for repos ([REPO] dir/)[cite: 3].
- PM_REPO_ROOT:     list of snapshots (.snap) of the opened repo[cite: 3].
- PM_SNAP_VIRTUAL:  content of the opened snapshot, navigable as
                    a virtual filesystem[cite: 3].
Keys[cite: 3]:
    F1          Help[cite: 3]
    F5          Backup (create): with AUTO-INIT if no repo is open[cite: 3]
    F6          Extract (extract)[cite: 3]
    F7          MkDir: create directory in active panel[cite: 3]
    F8          Prune: retention policy dialog[cite: 3]
    F9          Menu (File / Oper / Opc / Help)[cite: 3]
    F10         Exit[cite: 3]
    Ctrl+Q      Exit (alternative to F10 for terminals capturing F10)[cite: 3]
    ENTER       Enter directory / open repo / open snapshot[cite: 3]
    BACKSPACE   Go up one level (in active panel)[cite: 3]
    ESC         Back in right panel[cite: 3]
    TAB         Switch active panel[cite: 3]
    INS/SPACE   Mark/unmark item[cite: 3]
    Ctrl+C      Cancel ongoing operation[cite: 3]

NOTE: Ctrl+Q was added because terminals like MATE Terminal,

GNOME Terminal, and others capture F10 for their own window menu[cite: 3].

Auto-init with F5[cite: 3]:

When F5 is pressed without an open repo, the TUI offers to create one[cite: 3]:
1. Asks for new repo path (default: <current_dir>/backup)[cite: 3]
2. Asks for compression: LZ4 (default) or ZSTD[cite: 3]
3. If ZSTD: asks for level (1-22)[cite: 3]
4. Asks for encryption: yes/no + passphrase[cite: 3]
5. Creates the repo, opens it, and automatically performs the backup[cite: 3]

Simplified flow: TAB -> INS mark -> F5 -> Enter -> y -> Enter[cite: 3] (Note: translated s to y assuming Spanish 'sí')
F9 Menu > File > Initialize repo here[cite: 3]:

Also allows creating a repo manually with compression
selection (LZ4/ZSTD + level) and optional encryption[cite: 3].

Passphrase[cite: 3]:
When opening an encrypted repo, the TUI requests the passphrase in a hidden
input dialog (no echo, shows asterisks)[cite: 3]. It is kept in
memory, cleared with brs_secure_wipe on close, and passed to the
engine via BARESNAP_PASSPHRASE only during the operation[cite: 3].
Progress Popup and Terminal Compatibility[cite: 3]:

The TUI progress popup is drawn using direct fprintf to stderr
after executing endwin()[cite: 3]. This guarantees that if the operation involves an
SSH connection requiring TTY interaction (such as prompting for a password),
the terminal will not freeze[cite: 3]. Detection of Ctrl+C to cancel is performed
via poll() on STDIN_FILENO, avoiding the use of wgetch() which could
alter the terminal state[cite: 3]. Upon completion, refresh() re-enters
curses mode and redraws the panels[cite: 3].

============================================================================
10.1 INTERACTION ARCHITECTURE AND CONCURRENCY CONTROL
============================================================================
Starting from version 2.3.8, with the reduction of the pack footer size
to 33 bytes (u32 comp_size) and the introduction of mandatory synchronous
ACK flow control (BRS_RSP_OK), strict isolation is established between
the visual interface thread (Ncurses) and the asynchronous network threads[cite: 3].

============================================================================
10.2 MULTI-THREADED TRANSPORT ISOLATION (VFS SANDBOX)
============================================================================
Secondary worker threads do not share the global SSH descriptor context
of the interface thread[cite: 3]. The linear production design avoids
byte cannibalization on the STDIN channel[cite: 3].

+-------------------+-----------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------------+
| Thread            | Native Routine        | Context Control         | Transport Action                                                |
+-------------------+-----------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------------+
| Thread 1 (UI)     | run_with_progress     | nodelay(win, TRUE)      | Passively polls fd=0 (STDIN) without consuming the stream.      |
| Thread 2 (Net)    | backup_fn             | brs_vfs_context_clear   | Sets up a clean pipe with brs_vfs_open. Processes ACKs.         |
| Thread 2 (Net)    | extract_fn            | brs_vfs_context_clear   | Executes brs_repo_extract at 2.3ms/RPC without blocks.          |
| Thread 2 (Net)    | init_fn               | brs_vfs_context_clear   | Initializes remote structure and directory install.             |
+-------------------+-----------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------------+[cite: 3]

============================================================================
10.3 MEMORY ALLOCATION: HEAP VS STACK
============================================================================
Declaring flat infrastructure-sized buffers (>= 1 MB) within the Stack
in asynchronous routines is strictly prohibited[cite: 3]. Threads created with pthread_create
have a rigid stack limit in libc[cite: 3].

* Historical bug: The local array 'uint8_t buf[4*1024*1024]' in brs_remote.c
  caused a Stack Overflow, corrupting source pointers upon reaching 100% (SIGSEGV)[cite: 3].
* Mandatory mitigation: All reservation for transmission bursts must
  be requested in dynamic memory (Heap) via malloc() and explicitly freed
  with free() before performing pthread_join[cite: 3].

============================================================================
10.4 REMOTE VIRTUAL SYNCHRONIZATION IN EMPTY REPOSITORIES
============================================================================
When the remote SSH repository has 0 snapshots (newly initialized),
the right panel (ctx->right) activates a bypass free of blind local
checks like dir_is_repo()[cite: 3].

* Rescue Behavior (Fallback): Local getenv("HOME") usage is vetoed
  if the URI is remote (brs_uri_is_remote), protecting pointer consistency
  in RAM[cite: 3].
* Structure Result: Asymmetric paths are supported in parallel. Each
  local root directory generates an independent .snap file (index manifest),
  while unique chunks resulting from FastCDC chunking are stored
  as production immutable .pack files within the same global repository[cite: 3].

============================================================================
10.5 DIAGNOSTICS AND ERROR CODES
============================================================================

+------------------------+-----------------------+---------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+
| Error Code / Signal    | Component / Line      | Probable Cause                        | Production Action                                |
+------------------------+-----------------------+---------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+
| SIGSEGV (0x0 / NULL)   | brs_remote.c:strlen   | A local buffer has crushed the stack  | Verify that malloc() is used on the Heap and     |
|                        |                       | or global context has been cleared.   | not flat arrays on the Stack.                    |
+------------------------+-----------------------+---------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+
| ENOTDIR (opendir)      | brs_repo_create       | A regular file was passed (e.g.,      | Select a directory with INS or add a             |
|                        |                       | brs_tui.c) as root.                   | prior filter with stat().                        |
+------------------------+-----------------------+---------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+
| Deadlock / Freeze      | wgetch(win)           | The ncurses visual loop is reading    | Apply the passive filter with poll() on          |
|                        |                       | synchronous ACKs from the SSH socket. | descriptor 0 (STDIN).                            |
+------------------------+-----------------------+---------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+[cite: 3]

============================================================================
10.6 ABSOLUTE PATH RESOLUTION IN VIRTUAL DECOMPRESSION (F6)
============================================================================

When moving inside the `PM_SNAP_VIRTUAL` virtual view, the manifest index
entries are already given by the complete canonical path stored in the data
engine (`e->path`)[cite: 3]. 

*   Design Rule: The main TUI thread must never concatenate the prefix
    `p->current_path` onto the shared argument `j->paths[k]`. Attempting to join
    the navigation path results in a cloned Frankenstein path (e.g.: /aa/aa/file)
    that breaks the asynchronous network match, asserting a zero-elements error[cite: 3].
    The extraction command must be sent clean, strictly using the pointer
    `p->aux[i]` without modifications so that the network agent can house it properly[cite: 3].

============================================================================
10.7 STATIC METADATA REUSE (BYTE SIZES WITHOUT CPU LOAD)
============================================================================

To minimize system calls on limited infrastructure machines (Intel
Core 2 Duo or similar), the byte size visual format is coupled by taking advantage
of structures already housed in RAM stack during structural sweeping[cite: 3]:

    A) For Local Disk Files: Read from `st.st_size` (struct stat) [0.1.1, 26.1][cite: 3].
    B) For Remote Snapshot Files: Read from `e->size` (BrsManifestEntry) [0.1.1, 26.1][cite: 3].

+------------------------+-------------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------+
| Regular Element        | Ncurses Text Format String                      | Stack Reserved Size                    |
+------------------------+-------------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------+
| Log / Text File        | snprintf(disp, sizeof disp, "%-24s (%llu B)")   | char disp[BRS_PATH_MAX + 16] minimum   |
+------------------------+-------------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------+[cite: 3]

============================================================================
11.1 BUILD AND COMPILATION
============================================================================

Requirements[cite: 3]:

- musl-gcc (musl toolchain for static binaries)[cite: 3]
- ncurses 6.5 (compiled from source with musl-gcc)[cite: 3]
- zstd (compiled from source with musl-gcc)[cite: 3]
- yad (GUI only, runtime)[cite: 3]
- openssh-client (remote regression tests only)[cite: 3]

Compile[cite: 3]:
./compile.sh[cite: 3]
./compile_tsan.sh    # Compilation with ThreadSanitizer (concurrency audit)[cite: 3]

The compile.sh script[cite: 3]:
1. Verifies dependencies (musl-gcc, strip)[cite: 3]
2. Compiles static ncurses 6.5 with musl-gcc[cite: 3]
3. Compiles monocypher (cryptography)[cite: 3]
4. Compiles xdelta3 (delta encoding)[cite: 3]
5. Compiles zstd (static libzstd.a)[cite: 3]
6. Links the entire project into a single static binary[cite: 3]
7. Strips the final binary[cite: 3]

Compile for ARM64 (Raspberry Pi 3/4/5, Cross-compiling)[cite: 3]:
The project includes `compile_arm64.sh`, a script that downloads and uses
the musl.cc cross-toolchain automatically[cite: 3]. It does not require a real
ARM machine to compile, generating a ~1.0 MB static binary[cite: 3]:
    ./compile_arm64.sh arm64[cite: 3]

Embedded libraries[cite: 3]:

- monocypher: XChaCha20-Poly1305, Argon2[cite: 3]
- xdelta3: binary delta encoding[cite: 3]
- lz4: fast compression (inline code in brs_lz4.c)[cite: 3]
- zstd: high-ratio compression (static libzstd.a)[cite: 3]
- ncurses: TUI (static libncurses.a)[cite: 3]

11.2 REGRESSION TEST SUITE

Execute[cite: 3]:
BARESNAP=./build/baresnap ./mega_test.sh[cite: 3]
BARESNAP=./build/baresnap ./mega_test.sh --no-ssh      # If no SSH[cite: 3]
BARESNAP=./build/baresnap ./mega_test.sh --no-valgrind # Skip modules 104-106[cite: 3]

Suite structure[cite: 3]:
- Sections 01-47: Local tests (basic cycle, delta, encryption, health, etc.)[cite: 3]
- Section 48: Complete SSH suite (40 tests)[cite: 3].
- Sections 49-52: Extreme resilience (ENOSPC, concurrency, satellite, CRC32C)[cite: 3].
- Sections 53-55: SSH stress tests[cite: 3].
- Sections 56-61: Chaos engineering and filesystem abuse[cite: 3].
- Sections 62-64: Protections (lock, SSH timeout, index checksum)[cite: 3].

- Section 66: File Cache self-healing[cite: 3].
- Sections 67-69: Abuse batteries, CLI edge cases, and integrated final boss[cite: 3].

  * 104: Basic stress Valgrind (init/create/verify/restore) with 10 MB
    random + 500 files[cite: 3]. Automatic log audit (0 leaks / 0 errors)[cite: 3].
  * 105: Extreme memory torture Valgrind (10 MB base + 500 unique 10 KB
    files, impossible deduplication)[cite: 3].
  * 106: Aggressive prune Valgrind with AES-256-GCM + ZSTD level 3[cite: 3].
- Sections 107-110: REGRESSION OF THE 4 STRUCTURAL FIXES v2.3.5[cite: 3]:
  * 107: FIX F1 — Encrypted Frankenstein (lost config + encrypted snapshots)[cite: 3].
  * 108: FIX O1 — POSIX RAM Guardian (ZSTD Ultra with low memory)[cite: 3].
  * 109: FIX M1 — Corrupt parse without leaks or crash (goto parse_error)[cite: 3].
  * 110: FIX R1 — Remote agent, handle churn[cite: 3].
- Section 112: Resource control (deterministic lock, bash lock vs
create/prune)[cite: 3].

Valgrind modules are ACTIVE BY DEFAULT[cite: 3]. They are disabled with --no-valgrind[cite: 3].
The check_vg_log() helper parses "definitely lost:" and "ERROR SUMMARY:" from each
Valgrind log and emits [PASS]/[FAIL][cite: 3]. The vg_section_gate() helper applies the
execution gate (skip if --no-valgrind or if valgrind is not installed)[cite: 3].
