==============================================================================
20. BEST PRACTICES FOR CRON AND PRODUCTION AUTOMATION
==============================================================================

This section contains copy-paste ready scripts and configurations
for production systems[cite: 3]. All examples assume[cite: 3]:

- Binary in /usr/local/bin/baresnap (or adjust BARESNAP variable)[cite: 3]
- Repository in /mnt/backup/repo (or adjust REPO variable)[cite: 3]
- Dedicated "backup" user with read permissions on sources[cite: 3]

============================================================================
20.1 VOLATILE CREDENTIAL PROTOCOL (RAM-ONLY)
============================================================================

Storing keys or passphrases in plain text files on the hard drive,
regardless of POSIX permissions (600), is prohibited[cite: 3]. Storing the
passphrase in plain text is a blatant bad practice; if an attacker
compromises the machine (escalates to root) or dumps memory, backup
encryption falls immediately[cite: 3].

Automation: The production environment must use the kernel's or initialization
platform's secure storage subsystem (e.g., systemd-creds)[cite: 3]. The repository key is decrypted exclusively in
the volatile RAM of the running process during the Handshake phase
and physically destroyed from environment variables (unset) immediately
after initializing the cryptographic session[cite: 3].

Secure production script example (/usr/local/bin/baresnap-nightly.sh)[cite: 3]:

#!/bin/bash
set -uo pipefail
# ... [Standard path configuration] ...
# NEW SECURITY APPROACH: Ephemeral RAM injection via --pass-fd
# The passphrase travels through an anonymous pipe, never touching environment or argv.
CREDENTIAL_NAME="baresnap-repo-key"
if systemd-creds list | grep -q "$CREDENTIAL_NAME"; then
    PASS="$(systemd-creds cat "$CREDENTIAL_NAME")"
    "$BARESNAP" --pass-fd 3 create "$REPO" "$SOURCE" \
        >> "$LOG_FILE" 2>&1 3<<<"$PASS"
    unset PASS
else
    echo "CRITICAL ERROR: Security credential not found." >&2
    exit 2
fi[cite: 3]

Explanation: --pass-fd prevents the passphrase from appearing in argv/cmdline[cite: 3].
The fd is closed after reading and the buffer is cleared in cleanup[cite: 3].

Administrator Note: To configure this on the server once,
execute[cite: 3]:

    systemd-creds encrypt --name=baresnap-repo-key /dev/stdin -[cite: 3]

    (You type the key and press Ctrl+D. The encrypted result is saved in Linux's
    confidential credential system, bound to server hardware or systemd master keys)[cite: 3].

============================================================================
20.2 BASIC SCRIPT: DAILY BACKUP WITH LOG
============================================================================

File: /usr/local/bin/baresnap-backup.sh[cite: 3]
Permissions: chmod 750 /usr/local/bin/baresnap-backup.sh[cite: 3]
Owner: chown backup:backup[cite: 3]

    #!/bin/bash
    # baresnap-backup.sh - Daily backup with log
    set -euo pipefail
    # --- Configuration ---
    BARESNAP="/usr/local/bin/baresnap"
    REPO="/mnt/backup/repo"
    SOURCE="/home,/etc,/var/www"
    LOG_DIR="/var/log/baresnap"
    LOG_FILE="$LOG_DIR/backup_$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S).log"
    # --- Preparation ---
    mkdir -p "$LOG_DIR"
    # Load passphrase if repo is encrypted (using secure method)
    if systemd-creds list | grep -q "baresnap-repo-key"; then
        export BARESNAP_PASSPHRASE="$(systemd-creds cat baresnap-repo-key)"
    fi
    # --- Run backup ---
    echo "=== BareSnap backup: $(date) ===" >> "$LOG_FILE"
    echo "Repo: $REPO" >> "$LOG_FILE"
    echo "Source: $SOURCE" >> "$LOG_FILE"
    echo "---" >> "$LOG_FILE"
    if "$BARESNAP" create "$REPO" "$SOURCE" >> "$LOG_FILE" 2>&1; then
        echo "RESULT: OK" >> "$LOG_FILE"
        exit 0
    else
        RC=$?
        echo "RESULT: ERROR (code $RC)" >> "$LOG_FILE"
        # Send email alert if mail is available
        if command -v mail &>/dev/null; then
            mail -s "[BareSnap] ERROR in backup $(date +%Y-%m-%d)" \
                root < "$LOG_FILE"
        fi
        exit $RC
    fi[cite: 3]

Cron entry (/etc/cron.d/baresnap)[cite: 3]:
    # Daily backup at 03:00
    0 3 * * * backup /usr/local/bin/baresnap-backup.sh[cite: 3]

============================================================================
20.3 COMPLETE SCRIPT: BACKUP + VERIFY + PRUNE + ALERTS
============================================================================

This is the recommended production script. It backs up, verifies
integrity, runs prune with retention policy, and sends email alerts
if anything fails[cite: 3].
File: /usr/local/bin/baresnap-nightly.sh[cite: 3]
Permissions: chmod 750 /usr/local/bin/baresnap-nightly.sh[cite: 3]

    #!/bin/bash
    # baresnap-nightly.sh - Backup + Verify + Prune with alerts
    set -uo pipefail
    # --- Configuration ---
    BARESNAP="/usr/local/bin/baresnap"
    REPO="/mnt/backup/repo"
    SOURCE="/home,/etc,/var/www"
    ADMIN_EMAIL="admin@miempresa.com"
    LOG_DIR="/var/log/baresnap"
    LOG_FILE="$LOG_DIR/nightly_$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S).log"
    # Retention policy
    KEEP_LAST=5
    KEEP_DAILY=7
    KEEP_WEEKLY=4
    KEEP_MONTHLY=6
    # --- Functions ---
    log() {
        echo "[$(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')] $*" | tee -a "$LOG_FILE"
    }
    send_alert() {
        local subject="$1"
        if command -v mail &>/dev/null; then
            mail -s "$subject" "$ADMIN_EMAIL" < "$LOG_FILE"
        elif command -v sendmail &>/dev/null; then
            {
                echo "Subject: $subject"
                echo "To: $ADMIN_EMAIL"
                echo ""
                cat "$LOG_FILE"
            } | sendmail "$ADMIN_EMAIL"
        else
            log "ALERT (no mail available): $subject"
        fi
    }
    # --- Preparation ---
    mkdir -p "$LOG_DIR"
    PASS=""
    if systemd-creds list | grep -q "baresnap-repo-key"; then
        PASS="$(systemd-creds cat baresnap-repo-key)"
    fi
    log "=== BareSnap nightly: start ==="
    log "Repo: $REPO"
    log "Source: $SOURCE"
    FAILED=0
    # --- Step 1: Backup ---
    log "--- Step 1: create ---"
    if "$BARESNAP" --pass-fd 3 create "$REPO" "$SOURCE" >> "$LOG_FILE" 2>&1 3<<<"$PASS"; then
        log "create: OK"
    else
        log "create: ERROR (code $?)"
        FAILED=1
    fi
    # --- Step 2: Verify integrity ---
    log "--- Step 2: verify ---"
    if "$BARESNAP" --pass-fd 3 verify "$REPO" >> "$LOG_FILE" 2>&1 3<<<"$PASS"; then
        log "verify: OK"
    else
        log "verify: ERROR - INTEGRITY COMPROMISED"
        FAILED=1
    fi
    # --- Step 3: Prune with retention ---
    log "--- Step 3: prune ---"
    if "$BARESNAP" --pass-fd 3 prune "$REPO" \
        --keep-last "$KEEP_LAST" \
        --keep-daily "$KEEP_DAILY" \
        --keep-weekly "$KEEP_WEEKLY" \
        --keep-monthly "$KEEP_MONTHLY" \
        >> "$LOG_FILE" 2>&1 3<<<"$PASS"; then
        log "prune: OK"
    else
        log "prune: ERROR (code $?)"
        FAILED=1
    fi
    # --- Step 4: Info (summary) ---
    log "--- Step 4: info ---"
    "$BARESNAP" --pass-fd 3 info "$REPO" >> "$LOG_FILE" 2>&1 3<<<"$PASS" || true
    # --- Credential cleanup ---
    unset PASS

    # --- Final result ---
    if [ "$FAILED" -eq 0 ]; then
        log "=== RESULT: ALL OK ==="
        exit 0
    else
        log "=== RESULT: ERRORS FOUND ==="
        send_alert "[BareSnap] ERROR in nightly backup $(date +%Y-%m-%d)"
        exit 1
    fi[cite: 3]

Cron entry (/etc/cron.d/baresnap)[cite: 3]:
    # Complete nightly backup at 02:00
    0 2 * * * backup /usr/local/bin/baresnap-nightly.sh[cite: 3]

============================================================================
20.4 LOG ROTATION WITH LOGROTATE
============================================================================

File: /etc/logrotate.d/baresnap[cite: 3]

    /var/log/baresnap/*.log {
        daily
        rotate 30
        compress
        delaycompress
        missingok
        notifempty
        create 0640 backup backup
        dateext
        dateformat -%Y%m%d
    }[cite: 3]

This keeps 30 days of logs, compressed starting the second day[cite: 3].
Logs are named with dates (backup_20260821.log.gz) to facilitate
searching[cite: 3].

To force a manual rotation (test)[cite: 3]:
    logrotate -f /etc/logrotate.d/baresnap[cite: 3]

To check configuration without executing[cite: 3]:
    logrotate -d /etc/logrotate.d/baresnap[cite: 3]

============================================================================
20.5 ALERTS IF VERIFY FAILS (WITHOUT FULL SCRIPT)
============================================================================

If you only want to add periodic integrity verification
without modifying the existing backup script[cite: 3]:
File: /usr/local/bin/baresnap-verify-alert.sh[cite: 3]

    #!/bin/bash
    BARESNAP="/usr/local/bin/baresnap"
    REPO="/mnt/backup/repo"
    ADMIN_EMAIL="admin@miempresa.com"
    if systemd-creds list | grep -q "baresnap-repo-key"; then
        export BARESNAP_PASSPHRASE="$(systemd-creds cat baresnap-repo-key)"
    fi
    OUTPUT=$("$BARESNAP" verify "$REPO" 2>&1)
    RC=$?
    if [ $RC -ne 0 ]; then
        {
            echo "Subject: [BareSnap] ALERT: verify failed in $REPO"
            echo "To: $ADMIN_EMAIL"
            echo ""
            echo "Repository $REPO verify has failed."
            echo "Date: $(date)"
            echo ""
            echo "$OUTPUT"
        } | sendmail "$ADMIN_EMAIL"
    fi[cite: 3]
echo "Exit code: $RC"
            echo ""
            echo "Full output:"
            echo "$OUTPUT"
        } | sendmail "$ADMIN_EMAIL" 2>/dev/null || \
        mail -s "[BareSnap] ALERT: verify failed" "$ADMIN_EMAIL" <<< "$OUTPUT"
        exit 1
    fi
    exit 0

Cron entry (weekly verification, Sundays at 06:00):
    0 6 * * 0 backup /usr/local/bin/baresnap-verify-alert.sh

============================================================================
20.6 MONITORING WITH NAGIOS / ZABBIX / PROMETHEUS
============================================================================

BareSnap does not have a native "check" mode, but the exit code of verify
serves as a Nagios/Icinga check:
File: /usr/lib/nagios/plugins/check_baresnap.sh

    #!/bin/bash
    # check_baresnap.sh - Nagios plugin for BareSnap
    # Exit codes: 0=OK, 1=WARNING, 2=CRITICAL
    BARESNAP="/usr/local/bin/baresnap"
    REPO="${1:-/mnt/backup/repo}"
    if systemd-creds list | grep -q "baresnap-repo-key"; then
        export BARESNAP_PASSPHRASE="$(systemd-creds cat baresnap-repo-key)"
    fi
    OUTPUT=$("$BARESNAP" verify "$REPO" 2>&1)
    RC=$?
    if [ $RC -eq 0 ]; then
        echo "OK - BareSnap repo $REPO intact"
        exit 0
    else
        echo "CRITICAL - BareSnap repo $REPO corrupt (rc=$RC)"
        echo "$OUTPUT"
        exit 2
    fi

Nagios/Icinga configuration:

    define command {
        command_name    check_baresnap
        command_line    /usr/lib/nagios/plugins/check_baresnap.sh $ARG1$
    }
    define service {
        host_name             backup-server
        service_description   BareSnap Integrity
        check_command         check_baresnap!/mnt/backup/repo
        check_interval        1440
        max_check_attempts    2
        contact_groups        admins
    }

For Prometheus (via node_exporter textfile collector):

Add to nightly script:

    # Write metrics for node_exporter textfile
    METRICS="/var/lib/node_exporter/textfile_collector/baresnap.prom"
    SNAP_COUNT=$("$BARESNAP" list "$REPO" 2>/dev/null | grep -c '\.snap$')
    VERIFY_RC=$("$BARESNAP" verify "$REPO" >/dev/null 2>&1; echo $?)
    {
        echo "# HELP baresnap_snapshots Number of snapshots"
        echo "# TYPE baresnap_snapshots gauge"
        echo "baresnap_snapshots $SNAP_COUNT"
        echo "# HELP baresnap_verify_ok Verify result (1=OK, 0=FAIL)"
        echo "# TYPE baresnap_verify_ok gauge"
        [ "$VERIFY_RC" -eq 0 ] && echo "baresnap_verify_ok 1" || echo "baresnap_verify_ok 0"
    } > "$METRICS"

============================================================================
20.7 NAS / USB DRIVE BACKUP WITH RETRY
============================================================================

When the repository is on a NAS (NFS/CIFS) or a USB drive,
the mount may fail transiently. This wrapper retries the
mount before running the backup:
File: /usr/local/bin/baresnap-nas.sh

    #!/bin/bash
    set -uo pipefail
    MOUNT_POINT="/mnt/backup"
    MOUNT_DEVICE="//nas/backup"
    MOUNT_OPTS="credentials=/etc/baresnap/nas-creds,iocharset=utf8,uid=backup,gid=backup"
    MAX_RETRIES=3
    RETRY_DELAY=30
    BARESNAP="/usr/local/bin/baresnap"
    REPO="$MOUNT_POINT/repo"
    SOURCE="/home,/etc"
    # Verify that the NAS is mounted; if not, mount with retries
    is_mounted() {
        mountpoint -q "$MOUNT_POINT" 2>/dev/null
    }
    if ! is_mounted; then
        for i in $(seq 1 $MAX_RETRIES); do
            echo "Mount attempt $i/$MAX_RETRIES..."
            mount -t cifs "$MOUNT_DEVICE" "$MOUNT_POINT" -o "$MOUNT_OPTS" 2>/dev/null
            if is_mounted; then
                echo "Mount OK"
                break
            fi
            sleep $RETRY_DELAY
        done
    fi
    if ! is_mounted; then
        echo "ERROR: could not mount $MOUNT_POINT after $MAX_RETRIES attempts"
        exit 1
    fi
    # Verify that the drive has sufficient space (>1 GB free)
    FREE_KB=$(df --output=avail "$MOUNT_POINT" | tail -1 | tr -d ' ')
    if [ "$FREE_KB" -lt 1048576 ]; then
        echo "ERROR: insufficient space on $MOUNT_POINT (${FREE_KB} KB)"
        exit 1
    fi
    # Run backup
    if systemd-creds list | grep -q "baresnap-repo-key"; then
        export BARESNAP_PASSPHRASE="$(systemd-creds cat baresnap-repo-key)"
    fi
    "$BARESNAP" create "$REPO" "$SOURCE"

============================================================================
20.8 COMPLETE CRON ENTRIES (COPY AND PASTE)
============================================================================

File: /etc/cron.d/baresnap

    # /etc/cron.d/baresnap - BareSnap automation
    # Format: min hour day month day_of_week user command
    # Daily backup at 02:00 (backup user)
    0 2 * * * backup /usr/local/bin/baresnap-nightly.sh
    # Weekly integrity check (Sundays at 06:00)
    0 6 * * 0 backup /usr/local/bin/baresnap-verify-alert.sh
    # Monthly prune on the 1st at 04:00 (in case nightly didn't do it)
    0 4 1 * * backup /usr/local/bin/baresnap prune /mnt/backup/repo \
        --keep-last 5 --keep-daily 7 --keep-weekly 4 --keep-monthly 6
    # Verify repo disk is not full (every hour)
    0 * * * * backup test "$(df --output=pcent /mnt/backup | tail -1 | tr -d ' %')" -lt 90 \
        || echo "ALERT: backup disk at $(df --output=pcent /mnt/backup | tail -1)" \
        | mail -s "[BareSnap] Disk almost full" root

============================================================================
20.9 PRODUCTION CHECKLIST
============================================================================

Before putting BareSnap into production, verify:
[ ] Repository is on a dedicated disk/partition (not on /)
[ ] Disk has at least 3x the size of the data to back up
[ ] The "backup" user exists and has read permissions on sources
[ ] Security credential is configured via systemd-creds (NOT in plaintext)
[ ] Backup script has 750 permissions (not 777)
[ ] Cron uses the "backup" user, not root (least privilege)
[ ] logrotate is configured for /var/log/baresnap/
[ ] Alerts are configured if verify fails
[ ] A complete restore has been tested at least once
[ ] Prune has been tested with --dry-run before actual prune
[ ] Repo disk is NOT the same as the source disk
[ ] If repo is on NAS: mount has retries and timeout
[ ] There is a second offsite backup or on another medium (3-2-1 rule)
[ ] If password SSH is used: BARESNAP_SSH_PASSWORD is protected

============================================================================
21. COMPARISON WITH OTHER BACKUP TOOLS
============================================================================

+------------------------+------------------+----------+----------+----------+
| Feature                | BareSnap v2.3.8  | Restic   | Borg     | Kopia    |
+------------------------+------------------+----------+----------+----------+
| Language               | Pure C11         | Go       | Python/C | Go       |
| Static binary          | ~1.2 MB          | ~40 MB   | ~15 MB   | ~60 MB   |
| Runtime deps           | NONE             | none     | none*    | none     |
| Chunk dedup            | FastCDC          | CDC      | Buzhash  | CDC      |
| Available encryption   | XChaCha20 or     | AES-256  | AES-256  | AES-256  |
|                        | AES-256-GCM      |          |          |          |
| CPU impact w/o AES-NI  | LOW (ChaCha)     | HIGH     | HIGH     | HIGH     |
| KDF (Key Derivation)   | Argon2           | scrypt   | PBKDF2   | scrypt   |
| Delta encoding         | xdelta3 (local)  | NO       | NO       | NO       |
| Compression            | Mixed LZ4/ZSTD   | zstd     | lz4/zstd | zstd     |
| Entropy firewall       | YES (Stochastic) | NO       | PARTIAL  | NO       |
| Bloom Filter           | YES (disk)       | NO       | NO       | NO       |
| Remote backends        | Native SSH       | S3/SFTP  | SSH      | S3/B2    |
| Data Format            | Open/Doc.        | Open     | Open     | Open     |
| Peak RAM** (10GB/73k)  | 16-68 MB         | ~40-60 MB| ~35-45 MB| ~50-70 MB|
| Multi-platform         | Linux-only       | all      | Linux/Mac| all      |
| Fast SSH backup        | 0.35s            | ~2-4s    | ~2s      | ~3-5s    |
| SSH pack upload        | Agent + ACK      | SFTP/S3  | SSH      | S3/B2    |
| Post-upload            | YES              | NO       | NO       | NO       |
| verification           |                  |          |          |          |
| Production maturity    | New Project      | High     | High     | High     |
+------------------------+------------------+----------+----------+----------+

* Borg's standalone binary already includes its own embedded Python environment.

** BareSnap supports dual-crypto: XChaCha20-Poly1305 (default) and AES-256-GCM
   (selectable with --encrypt aes). The others only support one algorithm.

*** Allows native coexistence of LZ4 and ZSTD compressed chunks in the
    same pack pool (.7z style) resolving flags bit by bit.

**** Native SSH via lightweight agent (baresnap-remote) auto-deployable.
     Supports public key AND interactive password authentication.

***** SSH incremental backup with 2953 files and local index cache.

****** v2.3.8: remote agent pack upload with block-level ACK and
       remote size verification. Eliminates corruption in large SSH
       backups (>750 MB).

BareSnap is the ONLY tool with:

- Delta encoding with xdelta3 (32x savings on modified files)
- Disk Bloom Filter for index lazy loading
- ~1.2 MB static binary with zero dependencies
- 100% documented binary format
- SSH remote agent preserving POSIX semantics (hardlinks, rename)
- Password SSH authentication without external dependencies
- Local SSH index cache for ultra-fast incremental backups
- Automatic recovery of inconsistent repos (health --repair)
- Detection and purge of zombie temporaries after kill -9/ENOSPC
- Dual-crypto with selection at init (XChaCha20 or AES-256-GCM)
- Per-session mixed compression and stochastic entropy firewall in RAM
- Reliable SSH pack upload with block ACK and size validation

==============================================================================
22. FAQ - WHAT IT CAN AND CANNOT DO (FREQUENTLY ASKED QUESTIONS)
==============================================================================

This section answers the most common questions from those discovering
BareSnap for the first time. If you come from Restic, Borg, or Kopia, read this
before deciding if BareSnap is for you.

============================================================================
22.1 CAN I USE BARESNAP WITH SSH?
============================================================================

Short answer: YES, BareSnap has native support for SSH since v2.1.0,
and PASSWORD authentication since v2.1.3. Since v2.1.4, incremental
backups over SSH are 12.2x faster thanks to local index cache.

Unlike pure SFTP, BareSnap deploys a lightweight agent
(`baresnap-remote`) on the target server that communicates via an
efficient binary protocol over stdin/stdout.

Advantages of this approach:

- Preserves POSIX semantics: hardlinks, atomic rename, symlinks.
- Efficient: a single SSH process per backup session, not per chunk.
- Secure: agent is confined to the repository directory.
- Flexible authentication: public keys AND interactive password.
- Fast: local index cache for incremental backups (v2.1.4).

Usage with public keys (recommended for production):
    # 1. Configure SSH key (once)
    ssh-keygen && ssh-copy-id user@server

    # 2. Install the agent on remote (done once)
    baresnap remote install ssh://user@server:/path/to/repo

    # 3. Verify connectivity
    baresnap remote test ssh://user@server:/path/to/repo

    # 4. Use any normal command with the ssh:// URI
    baresnap create ssh://user@server:/path/to/repo ~/docs

Usage with password (without SSH keys):
    # BareSnap will ask for password interactively via internal askpass.
    baresnap remote install ssh://user@server:/path/to/repo
    baresnap create ssh://user@server:/path/to/repo ~/docs

Automation with password (tests, scripts):
    BARESNAP_SSH_PASSWORD="my_password" baresnap create ssh://user@server:/path/to/repo ~/docs

NOTE: For production, it is strongly recommended to use SSH keys
instead of passwords. The BARESNAP_SSH_PASSWORD variable is
intended for tests and punctual automation, not for production cron.

Requirements:

- SSH access to the server (by key or password).
- Remote user must have write permissions on the path.
- For password: server must have PasswordAuthentication yes.

============================================================================
22.2 CAN I BACK UP TO THE CLOUD (S3, B2, GCS)?
============================================================================

NOT directly in v2.2.0. BareSnap does not include HTTP clients,
WebDAV, S3, or cloud SDKs.

However, rclone integration is trivial and tested
in production. The flow is: BareSnap creates local repo, rclone
syncs it to the cloud.

RCLONE CONFIGURATION (once per provider):

Amazon S3:
    rclone config create s3 s3 \
        provider=AWS \
        access_key_id=AKIA... \
        secret_access_key=... \
        region=us-east-1

Backblaze B2:
    rclone config create b2 b2 \
        account=KEY_ID \
        key=APPLICATION_KEY

Google Cloud Storage:
    rclone config create gcs google cloud storage \
        project_number=123456789 \
        service_account_file=/path/to/key.json

ONE-LINE SYNCHRONIZATION:
    # S3
    rclone sync /mnt/backup/repo s3:my-bucket/baresnap --progress
    # B2
    rclone sync /mnt/backup/repo b2:my-bucket/baresnap --progress
    # GCS
    rclone sync /mnt/backup/repo gcs:my-bucket/baresnap --progress
    # WebDAV / Nextcloud
    rclone sync /mnt/backup/repo webdav:baresnap --progress
    # Any other rclone supported backend (40+ providers)
    rclone sync /mnt/backup/repo REMOTE:bucket/baresnap --progress

INTEGRITY VERIFICATION IN THE CLOUD:
    # Mount bucket as local filesystem (read-only)
    rclone mount s3:my-bucket/baresnap /mnt/s3repo \
        --read-only --vfs-cache-mode minimal &
    # Verify integrity with BareSnap
    baresnap verify /mnt/s3repo
    # Unmount
    fusermount -u /mnt/s3repo

AUTOMATION WITH CRON (backup + sync + verify):
File: /usr/local/bin/baresnap-cloud.sh

    #!/bin/bash
    set -uo pipefail
    BARESNAP="/usr/local/bin/baresnap"
    REPO="/mnt/backup/repo"
    SOURCE="/home,/etc"
    RCLONE_REMOTE="s3:my-bucket/baresnap"
    # 1. Local backup
    "$BARESNAP" create "$REPO" "$SOURCE" || exit 1
    # 2. Verify local integrity
    "$BARESNAP" verify "$REPO" || exit 1
    # 3. Sync to cloud
    rclone sync "$REPO" "$RCLONE_REMOTE" --progress || exit 1
    # 4. (Optional) Verify integrity in cloud
    mkdir -p /mnt/s3repo
    rclone mount "$RCLONE_REMOTE" /mnt/s3repo \
        --read-only --vfs-cache-mode minimal &
    MOUNT_PID=$!
    sleep 2
    "$BARESNAP" verify /mnt/s3repo
    VERIFY_RC=$?
    kill $MOUNT_PID 2>/dev/null
    fusermount -u /mnt/s3repo 2>/dev/null
    exit $VERIFY_RC

Cron entries:
    # Daily local backup at 02:00
    0 2 * * * backup /usr/local/bin/baresnap-nightly.sh
    # Cloud synchronization at 04:00
    0 4 * * * backup /usr/local/bin/baresnap-cloud.sh

NOTES:

- rclone is the only external dependency. Installed with:
  apt install rclone          # Debian/Ubuntu/Mint
  dnf install rclone          # Fedora
  brew install rclone         # macOS (if used outside Linux)

- The cloud repo is a byte-for-byte copy of the local repo.
  BareSnap doesn't know or care that it's on S3/B2/GCS.

- For restores from cloud, mount with rclone mount and use
  baresnap restore normally.

- 3-2-1 rule is met: 3 copies (local + cloud + another cloud),
  2 different mediums (disk + object), 1 offsite (cloud).

- If you only need cloud backup without BareSnap, use Restic
  or Kopia which have native S3/B2/GCS backends.

============================================================================
22.3 CAN I MIX LZ4 AND ZSTD IN THE SAME REPOSITORY?
============================================================================

YES. Starting from version v2.3.0, BareSnap supports dynamic compression per
snapshot. Although the repository is initialized with a default codec in
"baresnap init" (reading base from the global config file), you can force
a different algorithm for a specific backup session using session
environment variables directly in the console:

    $ BRS_CREATE_COMPRESSION=zstd BRS_CREATE_ZSTD_LEVEL=12 ./baresnap create ...

The engine will natively flag each individual chunk with its exact
combination of binary masks in the physical header of the PackEntry. In addition, the
entropy firewall will automatically intercept multimedia files
in RAM, turning off the compression bit if white noise is detected, optimizing
space and saving the CPU of your old 2007 processor.

============================================================================
22.4 HOW DOES THE ENGINE DETECT WHAT COMPRESSION EACH CHUNK USES?
============================================================================

The resolution is completely automatic, atomic, and bit-level. The engine
does not rely on global metadata to decode raw bytes. Strict constants
defined in 'brs_types.h' are evaluated directly in the hot path
of the restore, verify, and extract commands:

- If the block has the bit active (flags & BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_ZSTD), the pipeline
  invokes the libzstd decompression wrapper.

- If it has the BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED bit active but not ZSTD, the
  engine processes the fast stream and calls brs_lz4_decompress.

- If the entropy firewall determined the block was incompressible,
  the compression bit is turned off on disk and the engine skips that phase.

This allows logs snapshots in ZSTD-15 and code in LZ4 to coexist
in the same physical pool without interference.


============================================================================
22.5 CAN I CHANGE THE COMPRESSION OF AN EXISTING REPO?
============================================================================

NO. There is no automatic command (like 'convert' or 'migrate') to
recompress the history of a repository. The initial configuration is
hardcoded into the central 'config' file and old blocks are
immutable.

Starting from version v2.3.0, what you can do is change the compression
strategy for NEW backups of that same repository using
command-line session variables (e.g., forcing ZSTD over an LZ4 repo).

New data will use the new algorithm and old ones will remain as
they were, coexisting in the same pool.

If what you need is to change the algorithm UNIFORMLY for the entire
repository (including past backups), the manual procedure
remains the only path:

1. Create a new repo with desired compression in init.
2. Restore the snapshot of the old repo to disk (full restore).
3. Do 'create' from disk to new repo.
4. Verify structural consistency with "baresnap verify".
5. Delete the old repo.

============================================================================
22.6 WHAT HAPPENS IF THERE IS A POWER OUTAGE DURING A BACKUP?
============================================================================

The repo is NEVER left in an unrepairable state. Guaranteed by design:

- Packs are written as .tmp in tmp/ and only renamed
  to packs/ upon clean completion. A power outage leaves an
  orphan .tmp that the next create cleans up automatically.

- The snapshot (.snap) is written AT THE END, after all
  packs and the index. If the outage hits earlier, there simply is no
  new snapshot.

- The index uses write_file_atomic (write-to-tmp + rename).
- The file cache may become inconsistent, but the next
  create detects it and treats affected files as cache
  miss. Never causes incorrect data.

In the worst case: there is an orphan .tmp pack that cleans itself up.

Existing data is intact.

============================================================================
22.7 CAN I RESTORE A SINGLE FILE?
============================================================================

YES, with "extract":

    baresnap extract /repo snapshot.snap /tmp/destination path/to/file
    # Example: extract only .bashrc

    baresnap extract /repo snap.snap /tmp/out home/user/.bashrc
    # Extract a complete folder

    baresnap extract /repo snap.snap /tmp/out docs/project
    # Without paths: extracts the whole snapshot (equivalent to restore)

    baresnap extract /repo snap.snap /tmp/out

In TUI: open snapshot, mark with INS, F6.
In GUI: "Extract Files" menu.

============================================================================
22.8 DOES IT WORK ON WINDOWS OR MAC?
============================================================================

NO. BareSnap uses Linux-specific POSIX calls and is designed
exclusively for x86_64 and ARM64 (aarch64) architectures on Linux.

The strict use of calls like getrandom, utimensat, and /dev/tty rules out
macOS and Windows. This limits its viability as a universal corporate
tool for mixed fleets, but guarantees performance and
spartan simplicity in pure Linux environments (from a 2007 Core 2 Duo
up to a modern Raspberry Pi 5).

For Windows, use Restic. For Mac, use Restic or Borg.

============================================================================
22.9 WHAT IF I FORGET THE PASSPHRASE?
============================================================================

Data is UNRECOVERABLE. There is no backdoor, no recovery key,
no "security question". It is XChaCha20-Poly1305 encryption with
Argon2 (64 MB, 3 passes). Without the correct passphrase, the KVV does
not verify and the engine rejects the key.

Recommendations:

- Store the passphrase in a secure password manager or,
  preferably, use it via systemd-creds.

- If using BARESNAP_PASSPHRASE in cron, make sure the
  script containing it is backed up on another medium.

- Consider an unencrypted repo inside a LUKS disk if
  passphrase management is an operational risk.

============================================================================
22.10 CAN I BACK UP A WHOLE DISK (BLOCK DEVICE)?
============================================================================

NO. BareSnap operates at the FILE level (opendir/lstat/read).
It does not read block devices (/dev/sda). For that use dd, Clonezilla
or block-level rsync.

What you CAN do:

- Backup mount point: baresnap create /repo /mnt/disk
- Backup a dd image: dd if=/dev/sda | gzip > img.gz and
  then baresnap create /repo /path/to/img.gz

============================================================================
22.11 HOW MUCH SPACE DOES THE REPO NEED?
============================================================================

Rule of thumb: the repo needs between 1x and 1.5x the size of unique
data (after deduplication and compression).

Factors affecting size:

- Deduplication: identical files between snapshots are stored
  only once. Ideal for incremental backups.

- Compression: LZ4 ~10-30% savings on text. ZSTD 10 ~30-40%.
  Random data (photos, video) do not compress.

- Delta encoding: files <256 KB with small changes generate
  deltas of ~4 KB instead of full re-chunking (32x savings).

- Pack overhead: ~100 bytes per chunk (index + footer).

Real example (1.2 GB of source code, 3 snapshots):
Logical:  3.6 GB (3 x 1.2 GB)
Physical:  1.1 GB (dedup + LZ4)
Ratio:   3.27x

Real example of incremental pack (daily backup):

- Processed entries:    2,872
- Cache hits:             2,629 (98.2%)
- Cache misses:           49 (1.8%, new/modified files)
- Generated pack:         157 KB (161,515 bytes)
- Overhead per entry:   ~55 bytes (metadata + chunk ID)

This means a typical daily backup of ~3,000 files where
only 49 have changed generates a pack of barely 157 KB, demonstrating
the extreme efficiency of the deduplication + delta encoding system.

To check current usage: baresnap info /repo

============================================================================
22.12 CAN I RUN BARESNAP FROM A RESCUE LIVECD?
============================================================================

YES. It's the star use case. The binary is static (~1.2 MB),
dependency-free. Just copy it to the LiveCD and run it:

    # From a rescue USB with 200 MB of RAM
    cp /mnt/usb/baresnap /tmp/
    /tmp/baresnap restore /mnt/backup/repo snap.snap /mnt/disk/

    # If repo is encrypted, it will ask for passphrase
    BARESNAP_PASSPHRASE="key" /tmp/baresnap restore ...

It doesn't need apt, pip, or any installed package. Just a compatible
Linux kernel (any modern one).

============================================================================
22.13 CAN I USE BARESNAP AS A BACKUP SERVER FOR MULTIPLE MACHINES?
============================================================================

There is NO server mode or proprietary network protocol. Each machine must
run its own instance of BareSnap.

Alternative: each machine backs up to its own local repo, and then
rsync/rclone syncs to central storage. Or use native SSH

support so each machine points to a shared repository
on a central server:
    machine1: baresnap create ssh://backup-server:/repos/machine1 ~/docs
    machine2: baresnap create ssh://backup-server:/repos/machine2 ~/docs

============================================================================
22.14 WHY ARE SSH BACKUPS SO FAST?
============================================================================

In v2.1.4, multiple SSH-specific optimizations were implemented.
In v2.3.8, pack upload was fixed to prevent corruption in
large backups.

Current optimizations:

1. Local index cache
   .idx segments are cached in:
     ~/.cache/baresnap/<hash>/index/
   after the first download.

   Subsequent incremental backups load indexes from local
   disk instead of downloading them from remote.

2. Disabling delta over SSH
   Delta encoding requires reading chunks from remote packs, generating
   hundreds of RPCs.

   By default, it is DISABLED on SSH.

   Explicitly enabled with:
     BRS_SSH_DELTA=1

3. Write-back batching 4 MB
   Maintained for general VFS operations, such as index and bloom writing.

   Important v2.3.8:
   Packs are NO LONGER uploaded via VFS write-back.

   Pack upload uses the remote agent with block ACK.

4. Local temporary pack + agent upload
   Packs are written locally first.

   Then they are uploaded through the existing SSH channel using:

     BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK
     BRS_MSG_DATA (4 MiB blocks)
     ACK for each block

   This replaces the old "massive upload" of v2.1.4.

5. Removal of redundant fsync
   Fsyncs over SSH are "lazy" by default.

   In the remote agent, the final pack fsync is kept to
   guarantee persistence before responding OK.

Result:

- An incremental SSH backup with 2,953 files takes
  0.35 seconds (12.2x faster than v2.1.3).

- An 8.7 GB SSH backup with 40 packs and 258,747 chunks
  verifies correctly without corruption.

To diagnose slow SSH backups:

    BRS_SSH_DEBUG=1 baresnap create ssh://repo ~/data 2>&1 | grep "\[SSH"

This shows each individual RPC and the final summary.

To diagnose latency or adjust timeout:

    BRS_SSH_DEBUG=1 baresnap --timeout 120000 create ssh://repo ~/data

Timeout is controlled with global flag:

    --timeout <ms>

Default: 60000 ms.


============================================================================
23. SSH REMOTE BACKEND (VFS AND LIGHTWEIGHT AGENT)
============================================================================

Since v2.1.0, BareSnap natively supports remote repositories via SSH,
without depending on SFTP or FUSE mounts.

Since v2.1.3, supports PASSWORD authentication in addition to public
keys.

Since v2.1.4, incremental backups over SSH are 12.2x faster
thanks to local index cache and multiple latency optimizations.

Since v2.3.8, pack upload over SSH is reliable even in large
backups and pack rotations.

------------------------------------------------------------
ARCHITECTURE
------------------------------------------------------------

1. Client parses URI:

     ssh://user@host:/path/to/repo

2. The `remote install` command copies the `baresnap-remote` binary
   to the remote server at ~/.local/bin/

3. When executing any command, the client launches:

     ssh user@host "cd /repo && exec ./.local/bin/baresnap-remote --repo ."

4. Communication is performed via a binary protocol over
   stdin/stdout of the SSH process.

5. The VFS dispatcher (brs_vfs_dispatch.c) intercepts file system
   calls and routes them to the SSH backend.

------------------------------------------------------------
AUTHENTICATION
------------------------------------------------------------

- Public keys:
    Recommended method for production.

- Password:
    BareSnap generates an askpass wrapper at:

      ~/.local/libexec/baresnap-askpass

    which calls:

      baresnap --askpass-internal

    Password is read from /dev/tty with termios, without echo.

    For automation use:

      BARESNAP_SSH_PASSWORD

------------------------------------------------------------
PACK UPLOAD IN v2.3.8
------------------------------------------------------------

Before v2.3.8, large packs could be corrupted by VFS write-back
or uncontrolled massive uploads.

Now pack upload uses the remote agent with:

    BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK
    BRS_MSG_DATA (4 MiB blocks)
    ACK for each block
    pwrite in agent
    final fsync
    final size verification

Flow:

    Client -> Server: UPLOAD_PACK (name)
    Client -> Server: DATA (block 0)
    Server -> Client: OK
    ...
    Client -> Server: DONE
    Server -> Client: OK (total size)

If remote size does not match local:

    error: pack write failed (network I/O)

This eliminates silent corruption and external dependencies.

------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION OF EXISTING PACKS
------------------------------------------------------------

Before creating an incremental snapshot, create checks that all
packs referenced by the existing index still exist.

If a pack is missing:

    error: pack file 'packs/...' does not exist

And snapshot creation is aborted.

This prevents false snapshots generated by deceptive caching.

------------------------------------------------------------
MAINTAINED SSH OPTIMIZATIONS
------------------------------------------------------------

- Local index cache in ~/.cache/baresnap/<hash>/index/
- 4 MB write-back batching for general VFS operations
- Automatic disabling of delta encoding over SSH
- Removal of redundant fsyncs (lazy by default)
- RPC diagnostics with BRS_SSH_DEBUG=1

Note:
    Write-back batching is NOT used for packs.
    Packs use agent upload with ACK.

------------------------------------------------------------
ADVANTAGES OVER SFTP
------------------------------------------------------------

- Guaranteed atomic rename.
- Native hardlinks support (link).
- Single SSH process per backup session, not per chunk.
- Confined agent: rejects paths with ".." or absolutes.
- Efficient binary protocol.
- Blocking mode pipe: no EAGAIN or hangs.
- Handshake with poll(): early failure detection.
- Ultra-fast incremental backups via local cache.
- Reliable pack upload with block ACK (v2.3.8).

------------------------------------------------------------
REQUIREMENTS
------------------------------------------------------------

- SSH access to server.
- Remote user with write permissions.
- `baresnap-remote` installed via `remote install`.

- For password:
    PasswordAuthentication yes on server.

------------------------------------------------------------
PACKER ISOLATION AGAINST TOTAL DEDUPLICATION
------------------------------------------------------------

In incremental backups where 100% of data already exists
(total dedup), the engine counts new chunks.

If counter is 0:

    brs_pack_writer_abort()

Invoked cleanly and no empty packs upload is attempted.

------------------------------------------------------------
CONCURRENCY AND FINE-GRAINED MUTEX
------------------------------------------------------------

Global SSH connection mutex (h->ssh->mtx) is acquired and
released INSIDE the fread/fwrite loop.

This protects individual atomic RPC call without blocking the
scheduler, allowing continuous and stable network flow.

------------------------------------------------------------
ELASTIC PAUSE AGAINST SATURATION
------------------------------------------------------------

See section 23.1.

Adaptive Exponential Backoff mechanism is maintained.

In v2.3.8 it remains active and coexists with the new pack upload.

==============================================================================
23.1 ELASTIC PAUSE PROTOCOL FOR DISK SATURATION (NEW IN v2.3.6)
==============================================================================

In real-world massive backups, the continuous dump of heavy data packs 
can choke the remote server disk during synchronous fsync() flows. 
When the client's poll() method reaches zero, the engine no 
longer aborts the transfer rigidly.

The system activates an Adaptive Exponential Backoff mechanism:
  1. Pauses the local pipeline by stopping consumption of the lock-free SPSC queue.
  2. Backpressure safely freezes the FastCDC chunker and worker threads.
  3. Prints a warning on stderr: '[WARNING] Network/Disk saturated, pausing and
     retrying... (attempt X/3)'.
  4. Doubles current timeout (tmo *= 2) and sleeps the local thread for 
      2000 ms with usleep() to give oxygen to the remote hardware.
  5. Retries the read/write operation synchronously over the SSH channel.

The engine will tolerate up to 3 consecutive heartbeat stumbles and 
elastic retries before failing the operation, guaranteeing 
the indestructible survival of the backup in environments with saturated
mechanical disks.

SSH: loading full remote index in RAM...
SSH: 0 segments (0 cache, 0 downloaded)
warning: remote index segments not loaded; using blooms
dedup     [================>       ]  69%  1431/2068                                                
[PAUSE] Network saturated in read (1/3). Timeout -> 60000 ms, waiting 2s...>
pack      - ...                                                                                     
created snapshot: ssh://test@192.168.1.2/home/test/baresnap_bin_linux/snapshots/2026-09-04_04-42-51-762_76b14427.snap
entries: 2495
cache: 0 hits, 2068 misses
[STATS] Raw Input Data: 739.63 MB
[STATS] Packed Output Data: 235.29 MB
[STATS] Compression Ratio: 68.19%
[STATS] Processing Time: 86.337 s
[STATS] Real Throughput: 8.57 MB/s

==============================================================================
24. RESILIENCE AND PERFORMANCE
==============================================================================

BareSnap v2.3.7 has undergone an exhaustive battery of stress tests, fuzzing,
and memory analysis to ensure reliability in production environments. Detailed results
are documented in section 11 (Code quality and coverage).

SSH Performance (v2.1.4):

- Incremental backup of 2,953 files over SSH: 0.35 seconds
- 30 total RPCs, 82.9ms average latency per RPC
- 12.2x faster than v2.1.3 (4.3 seconds)
- Local index cache: 0 download RPCs in incremental backups

==============================================================================
25. C11 ENGINE API REFERENCE
==============================================================================

Document generated from BareSnap source code.
This document covers the public API of the C11 engine.

Reference files:
    brs_repo.h      - Repository operations (main public API)
    brs_types.h     - Base types, constants, magics, flags
    brs_index.h     - Global index, Bloom Filters, .idx segments
    brs_buffer.h    - Generic dynamic buffer
    brs_fsutil.h    - Path and filesystem utilities
    brs_chunker.h   - FastCDC and streaming chunker

============================================================================
25.1 BASE TYPES AND CONSTANTS
============================================================================

    BRS_FORMAT_VERSION          1       Binary format version
    BRS_CHUNK_ID_LEN           16      Chunk hash length
    BRS_UUID_LEN               16      UUID length
    BRS_KEY_LEN                32      Symmetric key
    BRS_CRYPTO_SALT_LEN        16      Argon2 salt
    BRS_HOSTNAME_MAX          256      Max hostname length
    BRS_PATH_MAX             4096      Max path length
    BRS_PASSPHRASE_MAX       1024      Strict limit for passphrase

NEW IN v2.3.8:

    BRS_PACK_ROTATE_BYTES      100 MB   Pack rotation
    BRS_FOOTER_ENTRY_SIZE      33       Pack footer entry
    BRS_BLOOM_BITS_PER_SEG     16 Mbits Bloom per segment
    BRS_BLOOM_NUM_HASHES       5        Bloom hash functions

NOTE:
    BRS_PACK_MAX_SIZE no longer defines real rotation size.
    Kept for compatibility, but actual rotation relies on
    BRS_PACK_ROTATE_BYTES.

============================================================================
25.2 KVV (KEY VERIFICATION VALUE)
============================================================================

Fixed 72 bytes layout:
    Offset 0-23:   nonce (24 bytes)
    Offset 24-55:  ciphertext (32 bytes)
    Offset 56-71:  MAC (16 bytes)

Constants:
    BRS_KVV_SIZE        72
    BRS_KVV_OFF_NONCE   0
    BRS_KVV_OFF_CT      24
    BRS_KVV_OFF_MAC     56

Validated at compile time with _Static_assert.

============================================================================
25.3 FORMAT MAGICS
============================================================================

All magics are 8 bytes. Compare with memcmp(x, magic, 8).

NEVER use strlen() on them.

    BRS_MAGIC_CONFIG        "BSCFG001"   Config file
    BRS_MAGIC_PACK          "BSPK0001"   Pack header
    BRS_MAGIC_PACK_FOOTER   "BSPKFT01"   Pack footer
    BRS_MAGIC_INDEX         "BSIX0001"   Index segment (.idx)
    BRS_MAGIC_SNAPSHOT      "BSSN0001"   Plain snapshot
    BRS_MAGIC_CACHE         "BSCACH01"   File cache
    BRS_MAGIC_SNAPSHOT_ENC  "BRSNAP2\x01" Encrypted snapshot

IMPORTANT:
    BRS_MAGIC_SNAPSHOT_ENC contains byte 0x01.
    Do not use strlen().

============================================================================
25.4 FLAGS
============================================================================

Repository/snapshot flags:
    BRS_FLAG_ENCRYPTED        0x0001    RepoConfig.flags
    BRS_FLAG_HARDLINK         0x0002    ManifestEntry.flags
    BRS_FLAG_DELTA            0x0004    ManifestEntry.flags

Chunk flags (ChunkLocation/PackEntry):
    BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_COMPRESSED 0x01      Compressed chunk (LZ4 or ZSTD)
    BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_ENCRYPTED  0x02      Encrypted chunk
    BRS_CHUNK_FLAG_ZSTD       0x04      ZSTD (otherwise LZ4)

Valid combinations:
    0x00: uncompressed, unencrypted
    0x01: LZ4
    0x02: uncompressed, encrypted
    0x03: LZ4 + encrypted
    0x05: ZSTD
    0x06: uncompressed, encrypted (rare)
    0x07: ZSTD + encrypted

============================================================================
25.5 DELTA ENCODING
============================================================================

    BRS_DELTA_MAX_SIZE         256 KB     Default threshold
    BRS_DELTA_MAX_SIZE_BINARY  256 MB     With --delta-binary
    BRS_DELTA_MIN_RATIO        2          delta_len*2 < new_len to use delta

============================================================================
25.6 KDF ARGON2
============================================================================

    BRS_KDF_DEFAULT_NB_BLOCKS  65536      64 MB
    BRS_KDF_DEFAULT_NB_PASSES  3

============================================================================
25.7 OPERATIONAL LIMITS
============================================================================

    BRS_PACK_ROTATE_BYTES   100 MB      Pack rotation
    BRS_CHUNK_UNCOMP_MAX     64 MB      Anti-OOM limit for decompression
    BRS_FILE_READ_MAX         4 GB      Read limit per file
    BRS_FOOTER_ENTRY_SIZE    33 bytes   Pack footer entry
    BRS_BLOOM_BITS_PER_SEG   16 Mbits   Bits per Bloom filter
    BRS_BLOOM_NUM_HASHES      5         Bloom hash functions

NEW IN v2.3.8:

    BRS_PACK_MAX_SIZE is replaced by BRS_PACK_ROTATE_BYTES.
    BRS_FOOTER_ENTRY_SIZE changes from 41 to 33 bytes.

============================================================================
25.8 ENUMS
============================================================================

BrsFileType:
    BRS_FILETYPE_DIR       = 1
    BRS_FILETYPE_FILE      = 2
    BRS_FILETYPE_SYMLINK   = 3
    BRS_FILETYPE_HARDLINK  = 4
    BRS_FILETYPE_OTHER     = 5

BrsCompression:
    BRS_COMPRESSION_NONE   = 0
    BRS_COMPRESSION_LZ4    = 1
    BRS_COMPRESSION_ZSTD   = 2

BrsHashAlgo:
    BRS_HASH_FNV1A_128     = 0
    BRS_HASH_XXH3_128      = 1
    BRS_HASH_BLAKE2B_128   = 2

BrsCipherAlgo:
    BRS_CIPHER_CHACHA20_POLY1305 = 0
    BRS_CIPHER_AES256_GCM        = 1

============================================================================
25.9 STRUCTURES
============================================================================

BrsChunkId:
    uint8_t bytes[16]       BLAKE2B-128 hash of original chunk

BrsChunkLocation:
    uint64_t pack_id        Container pack ID (uint64 LE)
    uint64_t offset         Payload offset inside pack
    uint32_t comp_size      Stored size (compressed/encrypted)
    uint32_t uncomp_size    Original size before compression
    uint8_t  flags          Chunk flags

BrsPackEntry:
    BrsChunkId chunk_id     Chunk hash
    uint64_t   offset       Payload offset
    uint32_t   comp_size    Compressed size
    uint32_t   uncomp_size  Original size
    uint8_t    flags        Flags

BrsRepoConfig:
    uint8_t  uuid[16]       Repository UUID v4
    uint64_t created_ns     Creation timestamp (ns)
    uint32_t chunk_min      FastCDC min (16384)
    uint32_t chunk_avg      FastCDC avg (65536)
    uint32_t chunk_max      FastCDC max (262144)
    uint8_t  hash_algo      BrsHashAlgo
    uint8_t  compression    BrsCompression
    uint32_t flags          0x01 = encrypted
    int      encrypted      0/1
    uint8_t  crypto_salt[16] Argon2 salt
    uint32_t kdf_nb_blocks  65536 (64 MB)
    uint32_t kdf_nb_passes  3
    uint8_t  kvv[72]        Key Verification Value
    uint8_t  zstd_level     1-22
    BrsCipherAlgo cipher_algo  0=XChaCha20, 1=AES-256-GCM

BrsManifestEntry:
    char        path            Relative path
    uint8_t     type            BrsFileType
    uint16_t    flags           BRS_FLAG_HARDLINK, BRS_FLAG_DELTA
    uint32_t    mode            POSIX permissions
    uint32_t    uid, gid
    uint64_t    size            Size in bytes
    uint64_t    mtime_ns, ctime_ns
    uint64_t    dev, ino
    char       *symlink_target  NULL if not symlink
    char       *hardlink_to     NULL if not hardlink
    BrsChunkId *chunks          Array of chunk IDs
    uint32_t    chunk_count, chunk_cap
    BrsChunkId *delta_source_chunks  Source chunks for delta
    uint32_t    delta_source_count, delta_source_cap

BrsSecureKey:
    uint8_t bytes[32]       Symmetric key

============================================================================
25.11 PUBLIC REPOSITORY API
============================================================================

int brs_repo_create(const char *repo_path, const char *source_spec,
                    const char *label, int delta_binary,
                    BrsProgressCallback cb, void *cb_user,
                    _Atomic int *cancel_flag);
    Creates an incremental snapshot.

    Parameters:
        repo_path    Repository path (local or ssh://)
        source_spec  Source directories separated by comma ("a,b,c")
        label        Optional label (can be NULL)
        delta_binary 0 = delta 256 KB, 1 = delta 256 MB
        cb, cb_user  Progress callback
        cancel_flag  Atomic cancellation flag

    Return:
        0 = OK
        1 = error
        2 = canceled

    Behavior:

    - If repo doesn't exist, it auto-initializes.
    - If config is missing but index/ exists, aborts with "inconsistent state".
    - If active SSH VFS context, delta encoding is disabled.
    - Dynamic compression via BRS_CREATE_COMPRESSION and BRS_CREATE_ZSTD_LEVEL.

    NEW IN v2.3.8:

    - Before reusing cache, checks that all packs referenced by
      existing index still exist.

    - If a pack is missing:

          error: pack file 'packs/...' does not exist

      and create aborts.

    - Pack upload over SSH uses remote agent with block ACK and
      final size validation, not VFS write-back.

int brs_repo_restore(const char *repo_path, const char *snapshot_name,
                     const char *target_path,
                     BrsProgressCallback cb, void *cb_user,
                     _Atomic int *cancel_flag);
    Restores a complete snapshot to target_path.

    Parameters:
        snapshot_name  Snapshot name or partial prefix

    Return:
        0 = OK
        1 = error
        2 = canceled

    Behavior:
    - No changes in v2.3.8.
    - Chunk reading remains over SSH channel with pread.

int brs_repo_extract(const char *repo_path, const char *snapshot_name,
                     const char *const *paths, size_t n_paths,
                     const char *target_dir,
                     BrsProgressCallback cb, void *cb_user,
                     _Atomic int cancel_flag);
    Selective extraction of specific paths.

    Parameters:
        paths     Array of paths relative to original source
        n_paths   Number of paths (0 = extract whole snapshot)

    Behavior:
    - No changes in v2.3.8.
    - Directories expand recursively.
    - Paths are sanitized automatically.

int brs_repo_verify(const char *repo_path,
                    BrsProgressCallback cb, void *cb_user,
                    _Atomic int *cancel_flag);
    Full integrity verification.

    Behavior:
    - No functional changes in v2.3.8.
    - Pack verification uses correct SSH VFS.
    - Checks maintained:
        CRC32C of each pack
        hash of each chunk
        presence of chunks in index and packs
        delta_source_chunks

int brs_repo_list(const char *repo_path);
    Lists repository snapshots.

    No changes in v2.3.8.

int brs_repo_prune(const char *repo_path,
                   int keep_last, int keep_daily,
                   int keep_weekly, int keep_monthly,
                   int keep_yearly, int dry_run,
                   BrsProgressCallback cb, void *cb_user,
                   _Atomic int *cancel_flag);
    Deletes old snapshots and rewrites packs.

    No changes in v2.3.8.

int brs_repo_info(const char *repo_path);
    Displays repository statistics.

    No changes in v2.3.8.

int brs_repo_diff(const char *repo_path,
                  const char *snap1, const char *snap2);
    Compares two snapshots.

    No changes in v2.3.8.

int brs_repo_ls(const char *repo_path, const char *snapshot_name,
                const char * const *prefixes, size_t n_prefixes,
                int long_fmt, int recursive, const char *match);
    Lists or searches inside a snapshot.

    No changes in v2.3.8.

============================================================================
25.12 PROGRESS AND CANCELLATION CALLBACK
============================================================================

typedef void (*BrsProgressCallback)(void *user, const char *phase,
                                    uint64_t current, uint64_t total);
Parameters:
    user     User pointer (cb_user)
    phase    "scan", "dedup", "pack", "restore", "verify"
    current  Current element
    total    Total elements (0 if unknown)

Can be NULL to disable progress.
All commands accept _Atomic int *cancel_flag.
If *cancel_flag != 0, operation is canceled cleanly.
Can be NULL to disable cancellation.

============================================================================
25.13 DATA COMMANDS (CREATE, RESTORE, EXTRACT)
============================================================================

See section 25.11 for complete signatures of brs_repo_create,
brs_repo_restore and brs_repo_extract.

============================================================================
25.14 VERIFICATION COMMANDS (VERIFY, INFO, DIFF)
============================================================================

int brs_repo_verify(const char *repo_path,
                    BrsProgressCallback cb, void *cb_user,
                    _Atomic int *cancel_flag);
    Full integrity verification:

    - CRC32C of each pack
    - Decryption and decompression of each chunk
    - Hash of each chunk compared to its ID
    - Chunks of each snapshot present in index and packs
    - delta_source_chunks present
    Return: 0 = intact, 1 = corrupt, 2 = canceled

int brs_repo_info(const char *repo_path);
    Displays repository statistics.
    Return: 0 = OK, 1 = error

int brs_repo_diff(const char *repo_path, const char *snap1, const char *snap2);
    Shows differences between two snapshots.
    Return: 0 = OK, 1 = error

============================================================================
25.15 MANAGEMENT COMMANDS (LIST, PRUNE, LOAD_KEY, LS)
============================================================================

int brs_repo_list(const char *repo_path);
    Lists all repository snapshots.
    Return: 0 = OK, 1 = error

int brs_repo_prune(const char *repo_path,
                   int keep_last, int keep_daily, int keep_weekly,
                   int keep_monthly, int keep_yearly, int dry_run,
                   BrsProgressCallback cb, void *cb_user,
                   _Atomic int *cancel_flag);
    Deletes old snapshots per retention policy.
    Rewrites packs to remove dead chunks.
    Cleans orphan .blm.
    Return: 0 = OK, 1 = error, 2 = canceled

    Behavior:

    - Local: 2 concurrent workers for rewriting.
    - SSH: 1 worker for pipe stability.
int brs_repo_load_key(const char *repo_path, BrsRepoConfig *cfg_out,
                      BrsSecureKey key_out);
    Loads config and derives key if repo is encrypted.
    Return: 0 = OK, 1 = error
int brs_repo_ls(const char *repo_path, const char *snapshot_name,
                const char *const *prefixes, size_t n_prefixes,
                int long_fmt, int recursive, const char *match);
    Lists or searches snapshot contents.
    Return: 0 = OK, 1 = error

============================================================================
25.16 INDEX AND BLOOM FILTERS
============================================================================

BrsIndexMap: ChunkId -> ChunkLocation hash map.

Open addressing, linear probing, load factor 70%.

int  brs_index_map_init(BrsIndexMap *m, size_t initial_capacity);
void brs_index_map_free(BrsIndexMap *m);
int  brs_index_map_put(BrsIndexMap *m, const BrsChunkId *id,
                       const BrsChunkLocation *loc);

                                          const BrsChunkId *id);

BrsU64Set: uint64_t set (for live packs, loaded segments, etc.).

.idx segments on disk:

                            const BrsIndexMap *map);
int brs_load_all_indexes(const char *repo_path, BrsIndexMap *out_map);

                           BrsIndexMap *map);

                                     uint64_t seg_id,
                                     BrsIndexMap *map);

                              BrsIndexMap *map);

NEW IN v2.3.8:

- `brs_write_index_segment` generates a single global index.
- Lateral per-pack indexes are no longer written during rotation.
- On SSH, .idx is uploaded with `brs_vfs_upload_pack` using remote agent.
- Existing pack validation added before reusing index.

Bloom Filters:

                   const BrsBloomFilter *b);

                   BrsBloomFilter *b);

void brs_bloom_set_free(BrsBloomSet *set);

NEW IN v2.3.8:

- `brs_bloom_save` also uses reliable agent upload on SSH.
- Bloom filters remain auxiliary; final decision is
  queried against real index.
