==============================================================================
26. REMOTE AGENT (BARESNAP-REMOTE)
==============================================================================

Document generated from baresnap-remote.c and brs_remote_protocol.h.
This document specifies the remote agent that runs on the SSH
server and processes BareSnap client requests.

============================================================================
26.1 OVERVIEW
============================================================================

baresnap-remote is a lightweight agent that communicates with the BareSnap
client via a binary protocol over stdin/stdout.

    CLIENT ----SSH----> STDIN  ----> baresnap-remote ----> filesystem
    CLIENT <---SSH---- STDOUT <---- baresnap-remote <---- filesystem
    STDERR <---- diagnostics (not protocol)

Features:

- STDOUT reserved 100% for binary protocol.
- All informative output goes to STDERR.
- Confined to repository directory.
- Zero external dependencies.
- Maximum 1024 simultaneous handles.
- 4 MB upload buffer.

NEW IN v2.3.8 — Reliable pack upload:

- Pack upload uses BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK.
- Agent writes each block with pwrite.
- After each block, responds BRS_RSP_OK.
- Upon completion, does fsync and responds with total size written.
- Client validates that size against local file.

This prevents corruption in large SSH backups and allows detecting
early network or disk failures.

Usage:
    baresnap-remote --repo /path/to/repo
    baresnap-remote --test

Agent automatically installs with:
    baresnap remote install ssh://user@host:/path/to/repo

============================================================================
26.2 ARCHITECTURE AND DATA STRUCTURES
============================================================================

static int g_handles[MAX_HANDLES];      /* MAX_HANDLES = 1024 */
    Array of file descriptors. -1 = free slot.

static char g_repo_path[4096];
    Absolute path of repository. All paths resolve relative.

static int g_no_sync;
    1 = disable fsync on close/upload. Enabled with BRS_REMOTE_NO_SYNC=1.

NEW IN v2.3.8:

- In handle_upload_pack, if g_no_sync = 0, fsync is executed before
  responding to client.

- In handle_write, if g_no_sync = 0, fsync is also forced before
  returning OK.

- Objective is to guarantee real persistence of packs and written data
  before confirming to client.

Therefore:

    BRS_REMOTE_NO_SYNC=0   -> fsync active (production)
    BRS_REMOTE_NO_SYNC=1   -> fsync disabled (tests/performance)

============================================================================
26.3 MAIN LOOP
============================================================================

Main loop is an infinite loop that:

1. Reads a message from stdin with brs_proto_recv_msg().
2. Dispatches according to message type.
3. Frees payload.
4. Repeats until BRS_MSG_BYE or EOF.
If brs_proto_recv_msg() fails (EOF, broken pipe), agent terminates.

============================================================================
26.4 HANDLE MANAGEMENT
============================================================================
static int allocate_handle(int fd);
Searches for a free slot, assigns fd, returns index.
FIX R1 (v2.3.5): rejects fd < 0 at origin (if (fd < 0) return -1).
Returns -1 if no free slots (MAX_HANDLES reached).

static int get_handle(int idx);
Returns fd of slot idx.
FIX R1 (v2.3.5): validates index AND validates fd >= 0 before returning.
If slot contains an invalid descriptor, returns -1 instead of
propagating corrupt fd to protocol.

static void release_handle(int idx);
FIX R1 (v2.3.5): defensive release of descriptors:
1) Validates table index (idx in [0, MAX_HANDLES)) before touching anything.
2) Extracts fd from table FIRST (slot to -1) and then operates. A double
   release can never execute fsync/close on the same descriptor.
3) Never does fsync/close on an already invalid fd (fd < 0).
4) fsync can legitimately fail with EINVAL/ENOTSUP on read-only descriptors
   or special files: not treated as fatal.
5) close is retried on EINTR. Any other errno indicates an already
   closed/invalid fd and no further action is taken on it.

Limit: MAX_HANDLES = 1024 simultaneous handles.
Associated test: section 110 of mega_test (handle churn, 12 tests).

============================================================================
26.5 PATH VALIDATION AND CONFINEMENT
============================================================================

static int build_path(char *out, size_t out_size, const char *rel_path);

Validation:
1. Rejects if rel_path contains ".."
2. Rejects if rel_path starts with "/"
3. Constructs: out = g_repo_path + "/" + rel_path
4. Rejects if result exceeds out_size

If validation fails: BRS_RSP_ERROR with errno EACCES "unsafe path".

============================================================================
26.5a SNPRINTF VALIDATION IN HANDLE_LIST (FIX V2.3.1)
============================================================================

Problem:
The second while loop of handle_list() builds the full path of
each entry via snprintf(ent_path, sizeof(ent_path), "%s/%s",
full_path, ent->d_name). If the combined path exceeds 8191 bytes,
snprintf silently truncates. Subsequent stat() operates on an
incomplete path, returning incorrect metadata or ENOENT. Backup
continues with corrupted or empty entries without reporting error to client.

Fix (v2.3.1):
Verify snprintf return value immediately after call:

- If n < 0 (encoding error) or (size_t)n >= sizeof(ent_path)
  (truncation), the function:
  1. Frees dynamic buffer (free(buf))
  2. Closes directory (closedir(dir))
  3. Sends error to client: brs_proto_send_error(STDOUT_FILENO,
     ENAMETOOLONG, "Remote path component too long")
  4. Returns immediately

Impact:

- Prevents silently corrupted backups due to long paths.
- Client receives explicit error and can abort cleanly.
- No changes to binary protocol (uses brs_proto_send_error).

Validation test:

- Smoke test handle_list: 31/31 tests passing.
- Mega test sections 88-92: Basic LIST, long names (255 chars),
  deep path (20 levels), 5000 files, remote SSH.

============================================================================
26.6 POSIX OPERATION HANDLERS
============================================================================

BRS_MSG_OPEN (0x01):
    Payload: path_len(2 LE) + path(N). Flags: READ/WRITE/APPEND/TRUNC/CREAT.
    Responds BRS_RSP_HANDLE with index.

BRS_MSG_CLOSE (0x02):
    Payload: handle(4 LE). release_handle(). Responds BRS_RSP_OK.

BRS_MSG_READ (0x03):
    Payload: handle(4 LE) + offset(8 LE) + length(4 LE) = 16 bytes.
    Uses pread(). Responds BRS_RSP_DATA.

BRS_MSG_WRITE (0x04):
    Payload: handle(4 LE) + offset(8 LE) + data(N) = 12 + N bytes.
    Uses pwrite(). Responds BRS_RSP_OK.

    NEW IN v2.3.8:
        If g_no_sync == 0, fsync(fd) is executed before responding OK.
        This guarantees real persistence in critical writes.

BRS_MSG_RENAME (0x05):
    Payload: from_len(2) + from + to_len(2) + to. rename(). BRS_RSP_OK.

BRS_MSG_UNLINK (0x06):
    Payload: path_len(2) + path. unlink(). ENOENT = success (idempotency).

BRS_MSG_LIST (0x07):
    Payload: path_len(2) + path. opendir/readdir. Excludes "." and "..".
    Format: count(4 LE) + [name_len(2) + name + mode(4) + size(8)] * count.

BRS_MSG_STAT (0x08):
    Payload: path_len(2) + path. stat(). Responds size(8) + mode(4).

BRS_MSG_HARDLINK (0x09):
    Payload: existing_len(2) + existing + newpath_len(2) + newpath. link().

BRS_MSG_MKDIR (0x0A):
    Payload: path_len(2) + path + mode(4). mkdir(). EEXIST = OK if dir.

============================================================================
26.7 SMART SSH PROTOCOL V2 (SYNC_INDEX, UPLOAD_PACK)
============================================================================

BRS_MSG_SYNC_INDEX (0x20):
    Payload: empty. Agent dumps all .idx from index/ directory
    into a single BRS_RSP_INDEX_DUMP message.
    Format: seg_count(4 LE) + [seg_id(8) + data_size(4) + data(N)] * count.
    Purpose: Client caches locally in ~/.cache/baresnap/<hash>/index/.
    FIX v2.3.2: Robust parsing of segment IDs using strtoull with
    errno validation (instead of sscanf), preventing silent corruption
    when IDs exceed LLONG_MAX (common with brs_now_ns() generating
    uint64_t). If parsing fails, ENAMETOOLONG is sent to client and
    operation is aborted cleanly.

BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK (0x21):
    Initial payload: name_len(2) + name. Then stream of BRS_MSG_DATA (4 MB max
    per block) until BRS_MSG_DONE. Agent writes in streaming with
    4 MB buffer. fsync on finish if g_no_sync == 0.

v2.3.8 — Upload Pack with flow control

History:
    Previous protocol lacked block ACK.
    With backups exceeding ~750 MB, SSH connection desynchronized
    and packs resulted corrupted.

Sequence:

    Client -> Server: BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK (name)
    Client -> Server: BRS_MSG_DATA (4 MiB)
    Server -> Client: BRS_RSP_OK (for each block)
    ...
    Client -> Server: BRS_MSG_DONE
    Server -> Client: OK (total size as int32)

Agent uses pwrite to write at absolute offset and fsync at end.

============================================================================
26.8 CLOSE MESSAGE
============================================================================

BRS_MSG_BYE (0xFF):
    Responds BRS_RSP_OK with status 0. Frees payload. return 0 (terminates).

============================================================================
26.9 MAIN DISPATCH
============================================================================

    switch (type) {
    case BRS_MSG_OPEN:        handle_open(flags, payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_CLOSE:       handle_close(payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_READ:        handle_read(payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_WRITE:       handle_write(payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_RENAME:      handle_rename(payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_UNLINK:      handle_unlink(payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_LIST:        handle_list(payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_STAT:        handle_stat(payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_HARDLINK:    handle_hardlink(payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_MKDIR:       handle_mkdir(payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_SYNC_INDEX:  handle_sync_index(payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK: handle_upload_pack(payload, length); break;
    case BRS_MSG_BYE:
        brs_proto_send_ok(STDOUT_FILENO, 0);
        free(payload);
        return 0;
    default:
        brs_proto_send_error(STDOUT_FILENO, EINVAL, "unknown message type");
        break;
    }

============================================================================
26.10 AGENT ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES
============================================================================

BRS_REMOTE_NO_SYNC
    If defined and not "0", disables fsync in release_handle()
    and handle_upload_pack(). Usage: BRS_REMOTE_NO_SYNC=1.
    Purpose: accelerate writes on slow NAS.
    Risk: on power outage, last writes may be lost.

v2.3.8 — Remote agent

BRS_REMOTE_NO_SYNC=0
    Forces fsync after each write and when closing handles.

BRS_REMOTE_NO_SYNC=1
    Disables fsync.

Recommended in production: 0.

============================================================================
26.11 SECURITY CONSIDERATIONS
============================================================================

Path confinement: build_path() rejects ".." and absolute paths.
Permissions: Agent runs with SSH user permissions.
Handles: Limit of 1024 (MAX_HANDLES). EMFILE if reached.
Payload: BRS_MSG_MAX_PAYLOAD = 64 MB. Larger payloads rejected.
Clean STDOUT: 100% reserved for protocol. Info to stderr.

============================================================================
26.12 INSTALLATION AND USAGE
============================================================================

Automatic installation:
    baresnap remote install ssh://user@host:/path/to/repo
    Copies binary to ~/.local/bin/baresnap-remote and verifies with --test.

Manual usage via SSH:
    ssh user@host "cd /repo && exec ./.local/bin/baresnap-remote --repo ."

Requirements:

- SSH access to server (key or password).
- Write permissions on repo path.
- PasswordAuthentication yes in sshd_config (if using password).

============================================================================
26.13 DIAGNOSTICS AND COMMON ERRORS
============================================================================

Agent does NOT write logs to disk. All output goes to stderr.
To view output: BRS_SSH_DEBUG=1 baresnap create ssh://...

Common errors:
    "unsafe path"       Path traversal detected (contains ".." or "/")
    "too many handles"  MAX_HANDLES reached (1024)
    "path too long"     Path > 4096 bytes
    "bad payload"       Payload too short or invalid
    "no index dir"      index/ directory does not exist (SYNC_INDEX)
    "upload interrupted" Upload canceled before DONE

==============================================================================
27. SSH BINARY PROTOCOL
==============================================================================

Document generated from brs_remote_protocol.h, brs_remote.c,
brs_remote.h and brs_remote_v3.h.

This document specifies the binary protocol used between the BareSnap
client and the baresnap-remote remote agent over SSH.

============================================================================
27.1 PROTOCOL OVERVIEW
============================================================================

Protocol operates over stdin/stdout pipes of SSH process.

CLIENT (baresnap)               SERVER (baresnap-remote)
==================              ============================
brs_remote.c                    baresnap-remote.c
writes to write_fd -----> stdin
reads from read_fd <----- stdout

Characteristics:

- Synchronous request/response protocol.
- Single SSH process per session.
- Global mutex protects each atomic RPC.
- Configurable timeout via --timeout <ms> (default 60000 ms).
- Timeout affects both binary protocol and initial SSH
  handshake via ConnectTimeout.
- Max payload: 64 MB (anti-DoS).

============================================================================
27.2 HEADER FORMAT (6 BYTES)
============================================================================

All messages (request and response) have a fixed 6-byte header,
followed by payload.

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD     DESCRIPTION
------  ------  --------  ------------------------------------
0       1       type      Message type (see section 27.3)
1       1       flags     Message flags (depends on type)
2       4       length    Payload size in bytes (LE)

Total header: 6 bytes (BRS_MSG_HEADER_SIZE)
Serialization: little-endian.
Payload limit: BRS_MSG_MAX_PAYLOAD = 64 * 1024 * 1024 (64 MB)

============================================================================
27.3 MESSAGE TYPES (CLIENT -> SERVER)
============================================================================

Protocol version: BRS_REMOTE_PROTO_VERSION = 1

POSIX operations (range 0x01-0x0A):
    BRS_MSG_OPEN      0x01    Open file
    BRS_MSG_CLOSE     0x02    Close handle
    BRS_MSG_READ      0x03    Read from handle
    BRS_MSG_WRITE     0x04    Write to handle
    BRS_MSG_RENAME    0x05    Rename file
    BRS_MSG_UNLINK    0x06    Delete file
    BRS_MSG_LIST      0x07    List directory
    BRS_MSG_STAT      0x08    Get metadata
    BRS_MSG_HARDLINK  0x09    Create hardlink
    BRS_MSG_MKDIR     0x0A    Create directory

Smart SSH Protocol v2 (range 0x20-0x30):
    BRS_MSG_SYNC_INDEX    0x20    Sync complete index
    BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK   0x21    Streaming pack upload
    BRS_MSG_DATA          0x22    Data block (for upload)
    BRS_MSG_DONE          0x23    End of stream (for upload)
    BRS_RSP_INDEX_DUMP    0x30    Response: index dump

Close:
    BRS_MSG_BYE       0xFF    Close connection

============================================================================
27.4 RESPONSE TYPES (SERVER -> CLIENT)
============================================================================

    BRS_RSP_OK        0x80    Successful operation
    BRS_RSP_DATA      0x81    Data read
    BRS_RSP_HANDLE    0x82    Handle assigned
    BRS_RSP_ENTRIES   0x83    Directory entries
    BRS_RSP_STAT      0x84    File metadata
    BRS_RSP_ERROR     0xFF    Error

============================================================================
27.5 OPEN FLAGS
============================================================================

Used in BRS_MSG_OPEN as flags byte in header.
    BRS_OPEN_READ     0x01    Open for reading
    BRS_OPEN_WRITE    0x02    Open for writing
    BRS_OPEN_APPEND   0x04    Append mode
    BRS_OPEN_TRUNC    0x08    Truncate if exists
    BRS_OPEN_CREAT    0x10    Create if not exists

Combinations:
    READ + WRITE = O_RDWR
    WRITE        = O_WRONLY
    (none)       = O_RDONLY

============================================================================
27.6 PAYLOAD FORMATS BY MESSAGE
============================================================================

BRS_MSG_OPEN (0x01):
    Request: path_len(2 LE) + path(N). Flags in header.
    Response: BRS_RSP_HANDLE with handle(4 LE).
BRS_MSG_CLOSE (0x02):
    Request: handle(4 LE). Response: BRS_RSP_OK with status(4 LE).
BRS_MSG_READ (0x03):
    Request: handle(4) + offset(8) + length(4) = 16 bytes.
    Response: BRS_RSP_DATA with N bytes read.
BRS_MSG_WRITE (0x04):
    Request: handle(4) + offset(8) + data(N) = 12 + N bytes.
    Response: BRS_RSP_OK with bytes_written(4 LE).
BRS_MSG_RENAME (0x05):
    Request: from_len(2) + from + to_len(2) + to.
    Response: BRS_RSP_OK.
BRS_MSG_UNLINK (0x06):
    Request: path_len(2) + path. ENOENT = success (idempotency).
    Response: BRS_RSP_OK.
BRS_MSG_LIST (0x07):
    Request: path_len(2) + path.
    Response: BRS_RSP_ENTRIES with count(4) + [name_len(2)+name+mode(4)+size(8)]*count.
BRS_MSG_STAT (0x08):
    Request: path_len(2) + path.
    Response: BRS_RSP_STAT with size(8) + mode(4) = 12 bytes.
BRS_MSG_HARDLINK (0x09):
    Request: existing_len(2) + existing + newpath_len(2) + newpath.
    Response: BRS_RSP_OK.
BRS_MSG_MKDIR (0x0A):
    Request: path_len(2) + path + mode(4). EEXIST = OK if dir.
    Response: BRS_RSP_OK.

BRS_MSG_BYE (0xFF):
    Empty payload. Response: BRS_RSP_OK. Agent terminates.

============================================================================
27.7 SMART SSH PROTOCOL V2 (SYNC_INDEX, UPLOAD_PACK)
============================================================================

BRS_MSG_SYNC_INDEX (0x20):
    Request: empty. Response: BRS_RSP_INDEX_DUMP (0x30).
    Payload: seg_count(4 LE) + [seg_id(8) + data_size(4) + data(N)] * count.
    Purpose: Sync all .idx in a single RTT.

BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK (0x21):
    Request: name_len(2) + name. Then stream:
    BRS_MSG_DATA (0x22): N bytes (max 4 MB per block).
    BRS_MSG_DONE (0x23): empty, signals end.
    Response (after DONE): BRS_RSP_OK with total_received(4 LE).

v2.3.8 — Changes in upload_pack

- BRS_MSG_DATA transports blocks of at most 4 MiB.
- Server responds BRS_RSP_OK for each block.
- Final response to BRS_MSG_DONE includes total size written.
- Client validates that size matches local file.

This prevents false uploads and silent cuts, especially in SSH
with backups over 750 MB.

============================================================================
27.8 ERROR FORMAT
============================================================================

BRS_RSP_ERROR (0xFF):

OFFSET  SIZE    FIELD       DESCRIPTION
------  ------  ----------  --------------------------------
0       4       errno_val   Error code (int32 LE)
4       2       msg_len     Message length (LE)
6       N       msg         Error message (no NUL)

Common values:
    2=ENOENT, 13=EACCES, 17=EEXIST, 22=EINVAL,
    24=EMFILE, 28=ENOSPC, 36=ENAMETOOLONG.

============================================================================
27.9 PROTOCOL V3 (HIGH LEVEL)
============================================================================

brs_remote_v3.h defines a high-level protocol coexisting with v2.
Currently NOT implemented in agent, but ranges are
defined for future implementation.

FIX v2.3.0: Opcodes migrated from 0x20-0x3F to 0x40-0x5F to eliminate
explicit collision with Smart SSH Protocol v2 (BRS_MSG_SYNC_INDEX=0x20,
BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK=0x21, BRS_MSG_DATA=0x22, BRS_MSG_DONE=0x23).

v3 messages (range 0x40-0x5F, no collision with v2):
    BRS3_MSG_GET_REPO_STATE 0x40, BRS3_MSG_PUT_REPO_UPDATE 0x41,
    BRS3_MSG_GET_PACK_DATA 0x42, BRS3_MSG_PUT_PACK_DATA 0x43,
    BRS3_MSG_LIST_DIR 0x44, BRS3_MSG_GET_FILE 0x45,
    BRS3_MSG_PUT_FILE 0x46, BRS3_MSG_DELETE_FILE 0x47,
    BRS3_MSG_MKDIR 0x48, BRS3_MSG_RENAME 0x49, BRS3_MSG_STAT 0x4A.

v3 responses (range 0xC0-0xDF):
    BRS3_RSP_REPO_STATE 0xC0, BRS3_RSP_PACK_DATA 0xC1,
    BRS3_RSP_FILE_DATA 0xC2, BRS3_RSP_SNAPSHOTS 0xC3.

NOTE: v2 ranges (0x01-0x0A POSIX, 0x20-0x30 Smart SSH) remain
free. V3 operates exclusively on 0x40-0x5F / 0xC0-0xDF.

============================================================================
27.10 TIMEOUT AND RETRIES
============================================================================

Timeout per operation: --timeout <ms> (CLI global flag).
Value is stored in global variable brs_ssh_timeout_ms
(brs_remote.c) and queried by ssh_timeout_ms() in each poll().

Examples:
baresnap --timeout 300000 create ...   # Satellite (5 min)
baresnap --timeout 60000  create ...   # Slow WAN (1 min)
baresnap --timeout 15000  create ...   # Fast LAN
baresnap create ...                    # Default: 60000 ms

Timeout applies PER POLL OPERATION. If there is activity
(data flowing), poll() returns before timeout.
Only aborts if socket is DEAD and unresponsive within deadline.

ELASTIC PAUSE WITH EXPONENTIAL BACKOFF (v2.3.6):
In massive backups, continuous dump can saturate remote disk.
When poll() expires on read (pr == 0), engine does NOT abort
immediately. Activates backoff protocol:

1. Prints warning on stderr:
   "[WARNING] Network/Disk saturated, pausing and retrying... (attempt X/3)"
2. Doubles current timeout (tmo *= 2).
3. Sleeps local thread 2000 ms with usleep() to give oxygen
   to remote server.
4. Retries poll() with new timeout.

Up to 3 consecutive retries allowed. If there is real read
progress (read() returns > 0 bytes), retry counter
resets and timeout returns to base value configured by CLI.
This prevents a punctual bump from leaving timeout inflated for the
rest of the transfer.

Retried errors: EINTR, EAGAIN, EWOULDBLOCK.
Fatal errors (no retry): POLLERR, POLLHUP, POLLNVAL,
                          Timeout after 3 elastic retries.
============================================================================
27.11 TYPICAL MESSAGE SEQUENCES (CREATE, RESTORE, VERIFY)
============================================================================

CREATE:
    C: OPEN config (READ) -> S: HANDLE
    C: READ config -> S: DATA (150 bytes)
    C: CLOSE -> S: OK
    C: SYNC_INDEX -> S: INDEX_DUMP (all .idx)
    C: UPLOAD_PACK name=tmp -> C: DATA* -> C: DONE -> S: OK
    C: OPEN idx (WRITE) -> C: WRITE -> C: CLOSE -> S: OK
    C: RENAME tmp->pack -> S: OK
    C: OPEN snap (WRITE) -> C: WRITE -> C: CLOSE -> S: OK
    C: BYE -> S: OK

RESTORE:
    C: OPEN snap (READ) -> C: READ -> C: CLOSE
    C: OPEN pack (READ) -> C: READ chunk -> C: CLOSE (for each chunk)
    C: BYE -> S: OK

VERIFY:
    C: OPEN pack (READ) -> C: READ complete -> C: CLOSE (for each pack)
    C: BYE -> S: OK

v2.3.8 — Remote create with pack upload

    C -> S: UPLOAD_PACK (packs/xxx.pack)
    C -> S: DATA (block 0)
    S -> C: OK
    ...
    C -> S: DONE
    S -> C: OK (size)

    C verifies size

    C -> S: WRITE (index/xxx.idx)
    C -> S: WRITE (index/xxx.blm)

Note: index and bloom are written via normal VFS, not upload_pack.

                     const void *buf, size_t len);

                    char ***names, size_t *count);

============================================================================
27.13 PROTOCOL ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES
============================================================================

BRS_SSH_DEBUG           1 = shows each individual RPC in stderr
BRS_SSH_STRICT_FSYNC    1 = real fsync on each operation (default lazy)
BARESNAP_SSH_PASSWORD    SSH password for punctual automation

NEW IN v2.3.8:

BRS_SSH_DELTA           1 = activate delta encoding on SSH
                        0 or absent = disabled (default)

NOTE (v2.3.6): BRS_SSH_TIMEOUT_MS removed. Timeout configured
by CLI with --timeout <ms> (default: 60000 ms).

BRS_REMOTE_NO_SYNC      0 = remote agent does fsync on writes/upload
                        1 = disables fsync (tests/performance only)

Recommended in production: BRS_REMOTE_NO_SYNC=0.

============================================================================
27.14 WIRE FORMAT: HEXADECIMAL EXAMPLES OF RPC MESSAGES
============================================================================

This section shows byte-by-byte packetization of real messages from
SSH binary protocol. With these examples, a third party can reimplement
the complete protocol in any language without reading C source code.

REPRESENTATION CONVENTION:
- Each byte is shown in hexadecimal, separated by spaces.
- Fields are grouped with | for visual clarity.
- LE = little-endian.
- Offsets are relative to message start.
- "..." indicates variable length payload.

HEADER LAYOUT (6 bytes, same for ALL messages):
+--------+--------+-------------------+-------------------------+
| Byte 0 | Byte 1 | Bytes 2-5 (LE)    | Bytes 6+                |
| type   | flags  | length (uint32)   | payload                 |
+--------+--------+-------------------+-------------------------+



============================================================================
EXAMPLE 1: BRS_MSG_OPEN — Open "config" for reading
============================================================================
Request: open repository "config" file in read mode.
Path: "config" (6 ASCII bytes: 63 6F 6E 66 69 67)
Flags: BRS_OPEN_READ = 0x01

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       01                      BRS_MSG_OPEN
flags       1       01                      BRS_OPEN_READ
length      2-5     08 00 00 00             8 payload bytes
path_len    6-7     06 00                   length of "config" = 6
path        8-13    63 6F 6E 66 69 67       "config" in ASCII

Raw bytes (14 bytes):
01 01 08 00 00 00 06 00 63 6F 6E 66 69 67

Visual breakdown:
[01][01][08 00 00 00]|[06 00][63 6F 6E 66 69 67]
 typ flg  length LE    p_len   p a t h

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 2: BRS_RSP_HANDLE — Response with assigned handle
============================================================================
Server assigns handle #3 to opened file.

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       82                      BRS_RSP_HANDLE
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     04 00 00 00             4 payload bytes
handle      6-9     03 00 00 00             handle index = 3

Raw bytes (10 bytes):
82 00 04 00 00 00 03 00 00 00

Visual breakdown:
[82][00][04 00 00 00]|[03 00 00 00]
 typ flg  length LE    handle=3

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 3: BRS_MSG_READ — Read 150 bytes from handle 3
============================================================================
Read 150 bytes (0x96) from offset 0 of handle 3.
Payload: handle(4) + offset(8) + length(4) = 16 bytes.

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       03                      BRS_MSG_READ
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     10 00 00 00             16 payload bytes
handle      6-9     03 00 00 00             handle = 3
offset      10-17   00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 offset = 0
read_len    18-21   96 00 00 00             read 150 bytes

Raw bytes (22 bytes):
03 00 10 00 00 00 03 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 96 00 00 00

Visual breakdown:
[03][00][10 00 00 00]|[03 00 00 00][00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00][96 00 00 00]
 typ flg  length LE    handle=3     offset=0                  len=150

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 4: BRS_RSP_DATA — Data read (150 bytes)
============================================================================
Server returns requested 150 bytes.

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       81                      BRS_RSP_DATA
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     96 00 00 00             150 payload bytes
data        6-155   42 53 43 46 47 30 30 31 "BSCFG001..." (content)
                    ...                     ... 150 bytes of config

Raw bytes (header, 6 bytes):
81 00 96 00 00 00 [150 bytes of data...]

Visual breakdown:
[81][00][96 00 00 00]|[42 53 43 46 47 30 30 31 ...]
 typ flg  length LE    file data

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 5: BRS_MSG_WRITE — Write 4 bytes at offset 1024
============================================================================
Write bytes "ABCD" (41 42 43 44) at offset 1024 (0x400)
of handle 3.
Payload: handle(4) + offset(8) + data(4) = 16 bytes.

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       04                      BRS_MSG_WRITE
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     10 00 00 00             16 payload bytes
handle      6-9     03 00 00 00             handle = 3
offset      10-17   00 04 00 00 00 00 00 00 offset = 1024
data        18-21   41 42 43 44             "ABCD"

Raw bytes (22 bytes):
04 00 10 00 00 00 03 00 00 00 00 04 00 00 00 00 00 00 41 42 43 44

Visual breakdown:
[04][00][10 00 00 00]|[03 00 00 00][00 04 00 00 00 00 00 00][41 42 43 44]
 typ flg  length LE    handle=3     offset=1024              data

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 6: BRS_MSG_CLOSE — Close handle 3
============================================================================
Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       02                      BRS_MSG_CLOSE
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     04 00 00 00             4 payload bytes
handle      6-9     03 00 00 00             handle = 3

Raw bytes (10 bytes):
02 00 04 00 00 00 03 00 00 00

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 7: BRS_RSP_OK — Successful operation
============================================================================
Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       80                      BRS_RSP_OK
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     04 00 00 00             4 payload bytes
status      6-9     00 00 00 00             status = 0 (success)

Raw bytes (10 bytes):
80 00 04 00 00 00 00 00 00 00

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 8: BRS_MSG_STAT — Stat of "config" file
============================================================================
Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       08                      BRS_MSG_STAT
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     08 00 00 00             8 payload bytes
path_len    6-7     06 00                   length = 6
path        8-13    63 6F 6E 66 69 67       "config"

Raw bytes (14 bytes):
08 00 08 00 00 00 06 00 63 6F 6E 66 69 67

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 9: BRS_RSP_STAT — Stat response
============================================================================
size=150 (0x96), mode=0100644 (0x81A4).

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       84                      BRS_RSP_STAT
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     0C 00 00 00             12 payload bytes
size        6-13    96 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 150 bytes
mode        14-17   A4 81 00 00             0100644 (regular file)

Raw bytes (18 bytes):
84 00 0C 00 00 00 96 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 A4 81 00 00

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 10: BRS_MSG_UNLINK — Delete "tmp/pack.tmp"
============================================================================
Path: "tmp/pack.tmp" (12 ASCII bytes).

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       06                      BRS_MSG_UNLINK
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     0E 00 00 00             14 payload bytes
path_len    6-7     0C 00                   length = 12
path        8-19    74 6D 70 2F 70 61 63    "tmp/pack.tmp"
                    6B 2E 74 6D 70

Raw bytes (20 bytes):
06 00 0E 00 00 00 0C 00 74 6D 70 2F 70 61 63 6B 2E 74 6D 70

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 11: BRS_MSG_MKDIR — Create directory "snapshots" (0755)
============================================================================
Path: "snapshots" (9 ASCII bytes). Mode: 0755 = 0x01ED.
Payload: path_len(2) + path(9) + mode(4) = 15 bytes.

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       0A                      BRS_MSG_MKDIR
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     0F 00 00 00             15 payload bytes
path_len    6-7     09 00                   length = 9
path        8-16    73 6E 61 70 73 68 6F    "snapshots"
                    74 73
mode        17-20   ED 01 00 00             0755

Raw bytes (21 bytes):
0A 00 0F 00 00 00 09 00 73 6E 61 70 73 68 6F 74 73 ED 01 00 00

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 12: BRS_MSG_RENAME — Move temp pack to definitive
============================================================================
from: "tmp/pack.tmp" (12 bytes)
to:   "packs/pack.pack" (15 bytes)
Payload: from_len(2) + from(12) + to_len(2) + to(15) = 31 bytes.

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       05                      BRS_MSG_RENAME
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     1F 00 00 00             31 payload bytes
from_len    6-7     0C 00                   from length = 12
from        8-19    74 6D 70 2F 70 61 63    "tmp/pack.tmp"
                    6B 2E 74 6D 70
to_len      20-21   0F 00                   to length = 15
to          22-36   70 61 63 6B 73 2F 70    "packs/pack.pack"
                    61 63 6B 2E 70 61 63
                    6B

Raw bytes (37 bytes):
05 00 1F 00 00 00 0C 00 74 6D 70 2F 70 61 63 6B 2E 74 6D 70 0F 00 70 61 63 6B 73 2F 70 61 63 6B 2E 70 61 63 6B

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 13: BRS_MSG_LIST — List "index" directory
============================================================================
Path: "index" (5 ASCII bytes).

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       07                      BRS_MSG_LIST
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     07 00 00 00             7 payload bytes
path_len    6-7     05 00                   length = 5
path        8-12    69 6E 64 65 78          "index"

Raw bytes (13 bytes):
Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       30                      BRS_RSP_INDEX_DUMP
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     14 00 00 00             20 bytes of payload
seg_count   6-9     01 00 00 00             1 segment
seg_id      10-17   2A 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 ID = 42
data_size   18-21   04 00 00 00             4 bytes of data
data        22-25   DE AD BE EF             .idx content

Raw bytes (26 bytes):
30 00 14 00 00 00 01 00 00 00 2A 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 04 00 00 00 DE AD BE EF

============================================================================
EXAMPLE 18: BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK + BRS_MSG_DATA + BRS_MSG_DONE
             WITH INTERMEDIATE ACK (v2.3.8)
============================================================================

IMPORTANT v2.3.8:
    The pack upload protocol has changed.

    Now, after each BRS_MSG_DATA, the server responds with
    BRS_RSP_OK as a reception confirmation.

    The client waits for this ACK before sending the next block.

    At the end, the server responds to BRS_MSG_DONE with BRS_RSP_OK
    including the total size received.

    This change eliminates corruption in large SSH backups.

Step 1 — Start upload of "tmp/pack.tmp":

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       21                      BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     0E 00 00 00             14 bytes of payload
name_len    6-7     0C 00                   length = 12
name        8-19    74 6D 70 2F 70 61 63    "tmp/pack.tmp"
                    6B 2E 74 6D 70

Raw bytes (20 bytes):
21 00 0E 00 00 00 0C 00 74 6D 70 2F 70 61 63 6B 2E 74 6D 70

Step 2 — Send first data block (4 bytes "ABCD"):

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       22                      BRS_MSG_DATA
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     04 00 00 00             4 bytes of payload
data        6-9     41 42 43 44             "ABCD"

Raw bytes (10 bytes):
22 00 04 00 00 00 41 42 43 44

Step 2b — Server ACK for the first block:

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       80                      BRS_RSP_OK
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     04 00 00 00             4 bytes of payload
status      6-9     00 00 00 00             0 = successfully received

Raw bytes (10 bytes):
80 00 04 00 00 00 00 00 00 00

(The client waits for this ACK before sending the next block.)

Step 3 — Send second block "EFGH" (if applicable):

22 00 04 00 00 00 45 46 47 48

And its ACK:

80 00 04 00 00 00 00 00 00 00

... repeated until all data is finished ...

Step 4 — Finalize upload:

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       23                      BRS_MSG_DONE
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     00 00 00 00             0 bytes

Raw bytes (6 bytes):
23 00 00 00 00 00

Step 5 — Final server response with the total size received:

Field       Offset  Hex                     Description
----------  ------  ----------------------  ---------------------------
type        0       80                      BRS_RSP_OK
flags       1       00                      no flags
length      2-5     04 00 00 00             4 bytes of payload
status      6-9     08 00 00 00             total received = 8 bytes

Raw bytes (10 bytes):
80 00 04 00 00 00 08 00 00 00

The client verifies that:

    remote_size == local_size

If they do not match, the upload is considered failed.

COMPLETE SEQUENCE: REMOTE CREATE (v2.3.8)
============================================================================

The following diagram shows the exact order of RPC messages during
a "baresnap create ssh://user@host:/repo ~/datos" with 1 new file.

  #  Direction  Message                      Description
  -  ---------  ---------------------------  --------------------------------
  1  C -> S     01 01 08 00 00 00 ...       OPEN config (READ)
  2  S -> C     82 00 04 00 00 00 ...       HANDLE = 0
  3  C -> S     03 00 10 00 00 00 ...       READ config (handle=0)
  4  S -> C     81 00 96 00 00 00 ...       DATA (config bytes)
  5  C -> S     02 00 04 00 00 00 ...       CLOSE handle=0
  6  S -> C     80 00 04 00 00 00 ...       OK

  7  C -> S     20 00 00 00 00 00            SYNC_INDEX
  8  S -> C     30 00 ...                    INDEX_DUMP (all .idx)

  -- Pack upload ----------------------------------------------------
  9  C -> S     21 00 ...                    UPLOAD_PACK "packs/<id>.pack"
 10  C -> S     22 00 ...                    DATA (pack block 0)
 11  S -> C     80 00 04 00 00 00 ...       OK (block 0 ACK)
 12  C -> S     22 00 ...                    DATA (pack block 1)
 13  S -> C     80 00 04 00 00 00 ...       OK (block 1 ACK)
      ... repeated for each 4 MiB block ...
 14  C -> S     23 00 00 00 00 00            DONE
 15  S -> C     80 00 04 00 00 00 ...       OK (total size received)

  -- Global index upload -------------------------------------------
 16  C -> S     21 00 ...                    UPLOAD_PACK "index/<id>.idx"
 17  C -> S     22 00 ...                    DATA (.idx blocks)
 18  S -> C     80 00 04 00 00 00 ...       OK for each block
 19  C -> S     23 00 00 00 00 00            DONE
 20  S -> C     80 00 04 00 00 00 ...       OK (total size)

  -- Bloom filter upload -------------------------------------------
 21  C -> S     21 00 ...                    UPLOAD_PACK "index/<id>.blm"
 22  C -> S     22 00 ...                    DATA (.blm blocks)
 23  S -> C     80 00 04 00 00 00 ...       OK for each block
 24  C -> S     23 00 00 00 00 00            DONE
 25  S -> C     80 00 04 00 00 00 ...       OK (total size)

  -- Snapshot write ------------------------------------------------
 26  C -> S     01 01 ...                    OPEN snapshots/<snap>.snap (WRITE)
 27  S -> C     82 00 ...                    HANDLE = 1
 28  C -> S     04 00 ...                    WRITE complete snapshot
 29  S -> C     80 00 04 00 00 00 ...       OK

  -- Close -----------------------------------------------------------
 30  C -> S     02 00 ...                    CLOSE handle=1
 31  S -> C     80 00 04 00 00 00 ...       OK
 32  C -> S     FF 00 00 00 00 00            BYE
 33  S -> C     80 00 04 00 00 00 ...       OK (agent terminates)

COMPLETE SEQUENCE: REMOTE RESTORE (7 messages)
============================================================================
The following diagram shows the exact order during a remote restore
of 1 file with 2 chunks.

  #  Direction  Message                      Description
  -  ---------  ---------------------------  --------------------------------
  1  C -> S     01 01 ...                    OPEN snapshots/snap.snap (READ)
  2  S -> C     82 00 ...                    HANDLE = 0
  3  C -> S     03 00 ...                    READ complete snapshot
  4  S -> C     81 00 ...                    DATA (.snap snapshot)
  5  C -> S     02 00 ...                    CLOSE handle=0
  6  S -> C     80 00 ...                    OK
  7  C -> S     01 01 ...                    OPEN packs/pack.pack (READ)
  8  S -> C     82 00 ...                    HANDLE = 0
  9  C -> S     03 00 ...                    READ chunk 1 (offset X, len Y)
 10  S -> C     81 00 ...                    DATA (chunk 1)
 11  C -> S     03 00 ...                    READ chunk 2 (offset Z, len W)
 12  S -> C     81 00 ...                    DATA (chunk 2)
 13  C -> S     02 00 ...                    CLOSE handle=0
 14  S -> C     80 00 ...                    OK
 15  C -> S     FF 00 00 00 00 00            BYE
 16  S -> C     80 00 ...                    OK

==============================================================================
28. SECURITY AUDIT AND APPLIED FIXES (V2.3.4)
==============================================================================
In the August 2026 audit session, 10 security and robustness bugs were 
identified in the source code. All were corrected and validated with functional 
tests (sections 93-103 of mega_test).

SUMMARY OF FIXES:
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+
| #   | Severity          | File                    | Fix                  |
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+
| 1   | CRITICAL          | brs_repo_prune.c        | Bounds check         |
|     |                   |                         | overflow-safe        |
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+
| 2   | CRITICAL          | baresnap-remote.c       | write_error detect   |
|     |                   |                         | + upload abort       |
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+
| 3   | CRITICAL          | brs_repo_restore.c      | goto pass_end +      |
|     |                   |                         | unlink(full)         |
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+
| 4   | MEDIUM            | brs_repo_create.c       | comp_buf free in     |
|     |                   |                         | join_and_fail        |
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+
| 5   | MEDIUM            | brs_vfs_dispatch.c      | tls/sftp rejected    |
|     |                   |                         | with clear error     |
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+
| 6   | MEDIUM            | brs_repo_create.c       | VFS cleanup with     |
|     |                   |                         | brs_uri_is_remote()  |
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+
| 7   | MEDIUM            | brs_repo_create.c       | VFS open with        |
|     |                   |                         | brs_uri_is_remote()  |
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+
| 8   | MEDIUM            | brs_repo_create.c       | #include brs_uri.h   |
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+
| 9   | MEDIUM            | brs_vfs_dispatch.c      | #include stdio.h     |
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+
| 10  | MEDIUM            | brs_repo_lock.h         | #error guard against |
|     |                   |                         | duplicate BrsRepoLock|
+-----+-------------------+-------------------------+----------------------+

ADDITIONAL DERIVED FIXES:
- Snapshot naming with milliseconds: prevents alphabetical collisions when
  two snapshots are created in the same second. The format changes from
  YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM-SS_hash8.snap to YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM-SS-mmm_hash8.snap.
- parse_snapshot_timestamp: corrected to look for '_' (underscore) instead
  of ' ' (space). Supports format with ms (23 chars) and without ms
  (19 chars) for compatibility with old snapshots.
- keep_by_period: period retention function restored. Necessary
  for prune --keep-daily/weekly/monthly/yearly. It had been lost after
  previous patches.

VALIDATION:
All 10 fixes are validated with functional tests in sections 93-103
of mega_test.sh (70 tests). Each test exercises the real bug scenario:
- Malicious offset injection in packs (Fix #1).
- Pack truncation and detection verification (Fix #2).
- Corruption of delta source packs and restore (Fix #3).
- Create with invalid path and temporary verification (Fix #4).
- Init with tls:// and sftp:// and rejection verification (Fix #5).
- Local→remote→local sequence with VFS (Fixes #6, #7).
- URI parsing and scheme errors (Fixes #8, #9).
- Serialized lock/unlock between processes (Fix #10).
- Integrated post-fixes regression (full cycle).

============================================================================
29. PROGRESS BARS IN CREATION AND EXTRACTION IN THE CLI
============================================================================

BareSnap displays progress bars during backup creation and
file extraction/restoration.

NEW IN v2.3.8 — Pack progress in create:

During `create`, in addition to the main `dedup` bar, clean messages
are displayed when creating and uploading each pack:

    [pack] Creating package 1...
    [pack] Uploading package 1 (5688 chunks, 100.16 MB)

    [pack] Creating package 2...
    [pack] Uploading package 2 (6429 chunks, 100.19 MB)

This allows seeing exactly how many packs are being generated and
uploaded, without the progress bar being cut off during rotation.

Extract example:

    [1/2] Downloading package 1/1 [==============================] 100%
     235.90 MB/235.90 MB 10.65 MB/s
    [2/2] Extracting files
    extract   [=============>          ]  54%  12234/22335

    [PAUSE] read stall off=0/6 base=30000 old_tmo=30000
    new_tmo=60000 retry=1/3
    >> Elastic pause acting as it should

    extract   [========================] 100%  22335/22335
    extracted 22347 item(s) to /home/mint/binlinux

Notes:

- Elastic pause remains active in v2.3.8.
- Pack messages do not interfere with the main bar.
- In SSH, pack upload uses the remote agent with per-block ACK.

==============================================================================
30. MANDATORY REQUIREMENT FOR TUI NAVIGATION UNDER SSH
==============================================================================
FLOW WARNING: If you use the visual interface (TUI) to connect to 
a remote repository via SSH, DO NOT use password authentication. 
Attempting to enter the password interactively causes the system prompt 
to break the Ncurses buffer, leaving the session hanging or cut off 
after the first extraction operation (F6).

To guarantee asynchronous 1.7ms/RPC performance without terminal 
interference, it is MANDATORY to configure secure key exchange:

1. Generate the key with an elliptic algorithm on your local Linux Mint:
   $ ssh-keygen -t ed25519

2. Copy the generated public key to the destination SSH server:
   $ ssh-copy-id user@server

Once this step is completed, BareSnap will open virtual VFS channels 
in the background without asking for passwords, ensuring flat, stable 
behavior and passing 100% of the megatest test suite.

==============================================================================
31. SSH CHANNEL PERSISTENCE POST-EXTRACTION (F6) IN THE TUI
==============================================================================
DESIGN BEHAVIOR: In the asynchronous thread architecture of version 
v2.3.8, decompression routines execute a strict closure of VFS channels 
upon finishing the physical download of chunks to prevent Memory Leaks 
in the server Heap.

*   Side Effect: Upon closing the secondary Worker channel, the active SSH 
    session descriptor of the visual panel is deemed extinguished by the 
    operating system. Attempting to navigate the remote tree immediately 
    afterwards will cause visual state corruption due to the lack of an active socket.

*   Standard Operating Procedure (SOP): Once a massive file extraction 
    is completed in SSH mode from the TUI, you must exit the program 
    (by pressing ESC or Q) and relaunch a clean connection. This ensures 
    that the engine negotiates a fresh new binary tunnel, clean of residual 
    buffers and with the native 1.7ms/RPC latency intact.


==============================================================================
32. CHANGELOG
==============================================================================

v2.3.8 (September 2026) + SSH STABILITY FOR PACKS AND ROTATIONS
        (brs_pack.c, brs_pack.h, brs_index.c, brs_repo_create.c,
         brs_repo_restore.c, brs_vfs_ssh.c, brs_remote.c, brs_tui.c):

  Byte Size Formatting and Canonical Synchronization (Iteration 137):
  * Visual Space Unification: The informative formatting has been extended to all three 
    TUI listing routines (`update_local` for the left panel, `update_picker` for 
    directory searching, and `update_virtual` for the inner snapshot view), forcing 
    column alignment with `%-24s`.
  * Production Size Integration: The libc `st.st_size` field via `lstat()` is reused 
    for physical disk files, and the canonical `e->size` member of the `BrsManifestEntry` 
    structure for snapshot files, painting precise sizes (e.g., `baresnap_tui.log         (1208 B)`) in the TUI.
  * Frankenstein Path Resolution in F6: Fixed a prefix duplication bug in the path loop 
    of `do_extract()`. By omitting the `p->current_path` prefix when the `e->path` (p->aux) 
    entry already contains the absolute canonical path of the manifest, 100% exact hash 
    matching via SSH is guaranteed, reviving files at 1.7ms/RPC and opening clean snapshots 
    with 25 RPCs in 0.222s.
  * Stack Visual Hardening: Preventatively expanded local text buffers `disp` and 
    `aux` to `BRS_PATH_MAX + 16` in listing headers, preventing byte formatting text 
    from overwriting return memory.


  Corrected writing and reading of the binary format of packs and indexes:
  * `comp_size` and `uncomp_size` are now `u32` (4 bytes) in writer, parser, and index.
  * `BRS_FOOTER_ENTRY_SIZE` changes from 41 to 33 bytes.
  * Prevents `checksum mismatch` and `cannot read pack footer` in multi-segment backups.

  Remote SSH mode:
  * Pack upload uses the remote agent again (`brs_remote_upload_pack`),
    eliminating the external dependency on `scp`.
  * An ACK is added for each 4 MB chunk for flow control and early detection
    of network drops.
  * The client verifies the final remote size before considering an upload successful.

  Rotation of packs and global index:
  * `BrsIndexMap *index_map` added to `BrsPackWriter`.
  * Rotations accumulate the entries of each closed pack in the global index.
  * `index_map` is preserved when reinitializing the writer (`memset`).
  * Fixes `MISSING chunks` when verifying repositories with multiple packs.

  Integrity verification:
  * Checks that all packs referenced by the index exist before
    reusing the cache and creating a snapshot.
  * Prevents false snapshots after accidental pack loss.

  Progress:
  * Shows `[pack] Creating package N` and `[pack] Uploading package N`
    instead of a progress bar getting cut off.

  Asynchronous TUI Interface and Memory Isolation (Iteration 133):
  * Fixed Critical Deadlock in SSH Networks: Secondary thread routines 
    (`backup_fn`, `extract_fn`, `init_fn`) were isolated through the explicit use 
    of `brs_vfs_context_clear()` and the opening of independent private VFS channels.
  * ACK Cannibalization Mitigation: Implemented passive filtering via 
    `poll()` on descriptor `0` (STDIN) in the TUI visual loop, preventing 
    `wgetch()` from devouring the remote agent's `BRS_RSP_OK` binary messages 
    and eliminating the need to perform a `pkill`.
  * Segmentation Violation (SIGSEGV) Solution: Fixed stack overflow in POSIX 
    threads by removing the 4 MB static buffer from the `brs_remote_upload_pack` 
    function. The buffer was relocated to dynamic memory (Heap) using `malloc()` 
    and `free()`.
  * Interface State Synchronization: Added network bypass in `open_repo()` 
    via `brs_uri_is_remote()`. Prevents desynchronization and erroneous Ncurses 
    jumping to the local HOME when initializing a remote repository with 0 snapshots.

  Symmetric Navigation and Relative Extraction (Iteration 136):
  * Regular File Visibility: Modified `update_picker()` function to remove 
    the exclusive directory filter `!S_ISDIR(st.st_mode)`. Flat files (including 
    internal logs and text files) now coexist in parallel alongside subdirectory 
    structures without altering RAM.
  * Elimination of Blind Virtual Descent: Extirpated the automatic `for(;;)` 
    auto-entry loop in `open_snapshot()`. Absolute symmetry with the CLI is restored, 
    allowing physical listing of the snapshot root directory (`aa/`) in the Ncurses 
    panel instead of teleporting the user's view.
  * Canonical Path Correction in F6: Redesigned path mapping in `do_extract()`. 
    By replacing the flat `strdup()` with a conditional formatter based on 
    `p->current_path`, the asynchronous engine receives the exact relative paths 
    indexed in the `.snap` manifest. Resolved the silent extraction anomaly.
  * Validated Network Performance: Tested asynchronous channel stability with 
    multi-RPC bursts (418 operations in 0.731s) operating at a latency of **1.7ms/RPC**, 
    guaranteeing clean Heap flushing without memory leaks.



v2.3.7 (September 2026) + DOWNLOAD PROGRESS IN EXTRACT (brs_repo_query.c 
        / brs_vfs_ssh.c):
  The `extract` command over SSH now shows a wget-style progress bar
  before starting to restore chunks, eliminating the "frozen screen"
  during massive pack downloads.
  * Phase [1/2]: "Downloading package X/Y [=====>    ] 45% 105 MiB/236 MiB 10.1 MiB/s"
  * Phase [2/2]: "Extracting files"

  Implemented via unique pack prefetch (`extract_prefetch_packs_cli`)
  and a new SSH read progress callback (`brs_vfs_ssh_set_read_progress`)
  that intercepts `brs_vfs_ssh_fread()` to report actual transferred bytes.

+ NEW GLOBAL FLAG: --pass-fd <N> (main.c):
  Reading passphrase from an anonymous file descriptor in RAM.
  The fd is closed immediately after reading. The passphrase
  NEVER appears in /proc/[PID]/cmdline or in argv.
  * g_pass_fd_buf: global buffer for secure_zero on cleanup.
  * read_passphrase_from_fd(): reading with EINTR handling,
    BRS_PASSPHRASE_MAX limit, newline stripping and \r. \n and \r.
  * cli_extract_pass_fd_flag(): extracting flag from argv
    with memmove (does not reach subcommand parsers).
* FD_CLOEXEC: descriptor is marked close-on-exec so child
processes do not inherit it.
  * cleanup_passphrase(): secure_zero + free + unsetenv,
    registered with atexit() to execute on any exit.
  Usage: baresnap --pass-fd 3 create /repo ~/docs 3<<<"$PASS"
  Usage: baresnap --pass-fd 3 create ssh://host/repo ~/docs 3<<<"$PASS"
  Compatible with --timeout and all subcommands.
Security note: avoids exposure in argv, ps aux and /proc/[PID]/cmdline.
Fd is closed after reading. Absolute invisibility in /proc/[PID]/environ
is not guaranteed if the engine reuses environment channels for compatibility.

+ RAM DEFENSES: THREE MEMORY FIREWALLS (main.c):
Formally documented in Section 12.1:
* SIGPIPE SIG_IGN: an SSH drop returns EPIPE instead of killing the process,
allowing cleanup, lock release, and passphrase deletion.
* FD_CLOEXEC in --pass-fd: prevents ssh/remote agent from inheriting the
passphrase fd during fork()+exec().
* secure_zero() with volatile: prevents GCC/Clang from eliminating the erasure
of sensitive buffers before free().
+ FIX: HARDLINK COUNTER IN EXTRACT (brs_repo_query.c):
  Fixed critical bug where `extract` progress exceeded 100%
  (e.g., 22347/22335). The two-pass hardlinks loop processed and counted
  links created in the first pass twice. Added `hl_done` bitmap
  (calloc) to mark already processed entries and avoid duplication in the
  `chunks_done` counter. Bitmap is correctly freed in `cleanup`.

+ FIX: MEMORY LEAKS IN EXTRACT DUE TO CHANGES IN 2.3.7rc1 ITSELF 
        (brs_repo_query.c):
  Multiple memory leaks detected by AddressSanitizer (ASan) have been
  fixed in the extraction path:
  * Removed double initialization of `brs_index_map_init()` which overwrote
    the pointer and lost ~1.5 MB of memory per execution.
  * Added freeing of `snap` (`brs_parsed_snapshot_free`) and `uncomp_buf`
    (`brs_buffer_wipe_free`) in the `cleanup` label.
  * SSH connection failure (`brs_vfs_open`) now uses `rc = 1; goto cleanup;`
    instead of direct `return 1` to ensure buffers (`data`,
    `chunk_buf`, `dec_buf`) are freed even if VFS fails to open.

+ SSH TIMEOUT IN INITIAL HANDSHAKE (brs_vfs_ssh.c / main.c):
  The global `--timeout <ms>` flag now injects `-o ConnectTimeout=X` into the
  `execlp("ssh", ...)` command during VFS opening (`brs_vfs_ssh_open`).
  Previously it only affected binary protocol read/write timeouts
  (`brs_proto_read_all`), ignoring bottlenecks in authentication, DNS, or initial TCP connection.

+ CLEANUP AND POSIX IN REMOTE AGENT (baresnap-remote.c):
  * Added macros `_POSIX_C_SOURCE 200809L` and `_XOPEN_SOURCE 700` at the beginning
    of the file to resolve implicit declaration warnings in `pread()`
    and `pwrite()`, ensuring strict compatibility with musl and glibc.
  * Removed dead function `parse_idx_seg_id()` (replaced by
    direct `strtoull` in `handle_sync_index`), cleaning up compilation
    warnings (`-Wunused-function`) and reducing code surface.

+ VFS CONTEXT AND TUI STABILITY (brs_repo_query.c / brs_repo_restore.c):
  Reviewed and hardened the `owns_vfs` logic in `brs_repo_extract()`,
  `brs_repo_info()` and `brs_repo_restore()`. If the frontend (CLI or TUI) has
  already opened the VFS context, query functions reuse it and DO NOT close it
  upon completion. This prevents double-frees and heap corruptions
  that caused segfaults in ncurses (`_nc_free_keytry`) when exiting the TUI with F10 after an extraction operation.

+ FIX: MISLEADING INDENTATION IN SSH (brs_vfs_ssh.c):
  Fixed `-Wmisleading-indentation` warnings in `brs_vfs_ssh_open()` and
  `brs_vfs_ssh_fread()`. Explicit braces `{}` have been added to `if` blocks
  managing pipes and progress callbacks to prevent the compiler from assuming
  that local variable declarations (like `size_t total`) were conditioned on the `if`.

+ CRITICAL FIX: MUTUAL EXCLUSION IN CREATE (brs_repo_create.c):
  Injected `brs_repo_lock_acquire(&repo_lock, repo_path, "create")` at the beginning
  of `brs_repo_create_impl()` and `brs_repo_lock_release(&repo_lock)` at the
  `cleanup` label, replicating the exact symmetry of `brs_repo_prune.c`.
  The `create` command completely ignored the repository lock,
  allowing two processes to create snapshots simultaneously and corrupt
  the index. The lock is acquired before any resource allocation
  (VFS, buffers, threads) and released on all exit paths:
  * Success (`rc = 0; cleanup:`)
  * Early error (`split_sources` → `goto cleanup`)
  * Cancellation (`check_cancel` → `goto cleanup`)
  * Pipeline failure (`join_and_fail` → `goto cleanup`)
  * Remote VFS failure (explicit release before `return 1`)

+ TEST SUITE: 9 NEW ASSERTS AND CONCURRENCY HARDENING:
  * Test 62 (Lock File): rewritten with stdout/stderr capture per process,
    lock failure vs unexpected failure classification, and `wait` protected
    with `|| RC=$?` to prevent aborts under `set -e`.
  * Test 75 (Concurrent Swarm): rewritten with Bash arrays, individual
    PID waiting, ok/locked/unexpected classification, and diagnostic output
    of each process's logs in case of non-lock failure.
  * Test 112 (Resource Control): converted to deterministic. The lock is
    retained by the shell itself during asserts (without background processes with
    `sleep` that die at the limit). Verified that a second descriptor
    CANNOT acquire the lock before launching `create`/`prune`. Opened in
    append mode (`>>`) to avoid truncating metadata. Post-test release checked.
  * Total counter: 926 → 935 regression tests.

+ Binary: ~1.2 MB (no significant size changes).
+ Suite: 935/935 regression tests passing (GCC and musl).


v2.3.6 (September 2026) — Elastic Pause + SSH Timeout by CLI
+ ELASTIC PAUSE PROTOCOL (brs_remote.c): When poll() expires
  on read during a massive SSH transfer, the engine no longer
  aborts immediately. It activates an Adaptive Exponential Backoff
  mechanism with up to 3 consecutive retries:
  * Warning on stderr: "[WARNING] Network/Disk saturated..."
  * Doubles current timeout (tmo *= 2).
  * Sleeps 2000 ms with usleep() to give oxygen to the remote disk.
  * Retries operation.
  * If there is real progress, resets counter and base timeout.
  Measured real impact: 739 MB backup that aborted at 69% due
  to disk saturation now completes in ~160 s with 1 successful
  elastic retry. Section 2.3.1 of the manual.
+ SSH TIMEOUT BY CLI FLAG (main.c / brs_remote.c): Removed
  dependency on the BRS_SSH_TIMEOUT_MS environment variable. New
  global flag --timeout <ms> extracted before command dispatch,
  working in any position on the line:
  * baresnap create --timeout 90000 ssh://... ~/docs
  * baresnap --timeout 90000 create ssh://... ~/docs
  * baresnap info --timeout 15000 ssh://...
  Default: 60000 ms (1 minute). Value stored in global variable
  brs_ssh_timeout_ms and queried by ssh_timeout_ms().
  Sections 8, 13.2, 14, 27.10, 27.13 updated.
+ UPDATED HELP: All print_help_*() in main.c now include
  examples with --timeout in remote SSH contexts.
+ ROBUST VALIDATION: parse_timeout_value() detects negative
  values, zero, overflow, and invalid formats with clear error
  messages before aborting.
+ Binary: ~1.2 MB (no significant size changes).
+ Suite: 926 regression tests continue passing (Elastic Pause
  is transparent to existing tests using short timeouts).


v2.3.5 (September 2026) — 4 Structural Fixes + Integrated Valgrind Modules
+ FIX F1 (src/brs_repo_health.c): brs_health_repair detects encrypted
  snapshots BEFORE recreating a lost config. New function
  health_detect_encrypted_snapshots() scans the BRSNAP2\x01 magic and marks
  the recreated config with encrypted=1. Prevents silent corruption in
  Frankenstein encrypted repos. Test 107 (11 tests).
+ FIX O1 (src/brs_repo_create.c): POSIX RAM Guardian in
  brs_resolve_session_compression(). If available memory is < 600 MB
  (BRS_RAM_GUARD_THRESHOLD_BYTES), degrades ZSTD Ultra (levels 20-22) to
  level 19 (BRS_ZSTD_SAFE_LEVEL) to mitigate OOM. Reads /proc/meminfo with
  fallback to sysconf(_SC_AVPHYS_PAGES). QA seam: BRS_RAM_GUARD_FAKE_MB.
  Test 108 (8 tests).
+ FIX M1 (src/brs_manifest.c): brs_parse_snapshot() refactored to
  centralized cleanup with goto parse_error label. Zero leaks of
  hostname/root_path/entries/chunks and wipe of decrypted plaintext in
  all failure paths. Test 109 (7 tests, with Valgrind).
+ FIX R1 (baresnap-remote.c): allocate_handle/get_handle/release_handle
  hardened. Fd extraction before operating (immune to double
  free), fd >= 0 validation, EINVAL-tolerant fsync, EINTR retry
  on close. Test 110 (12 tests).
+ VALGRIND MODULES INTEGRATED in mega_test (sections 104-106):
  * 104: basic stress init/create/verify/restore (10 MB + 500 files).
  * 105: extreme memory torture (10 MB + 500 unique 10 KB files).
  * 106: aggressive prune with AES-256-GCM + ZSTD level 3.
  Helpers: check_vg_log() (log auditing) and vg_section_gate().
  Active BY DEFAULT; disabled with --no-valgrind.
+ REGRESSION SECTIONS 107-110 for the 4 structural fixes.
+ Suite expanded to 926 tests: 926/926 passing in 6m 40s.
+ Valgrind: 0 leaks / 0 errors in all integrated modules.
+ ASan/UBSan: 58/58 tests, zero memory errors.
+ Binary: ~1.2 MB (no size changes).
+ NEW CONSTANTS: BRS_RAM_GUARD_THRESHOLD_BYTES (600 MB),
  BRS_ZSTD_ULTRA_MIN_LEVEL (20), BRS_ZSTD_SAFE_LEVEL (19).
+ NEW ENVIRONMENT VARIABLE: BRS_RAM_GUARD_FAKE_MB (QA/test of the guardian).

v2.3.3 (August 2026) — Security Audit: 10 Critical Fixes
+ COMPLETE SECURITY AUDIT: 10 bugs identified and fixed:
  1. Integer overflow in prune_worker(): bounds check overflow-safe
     (entries[e].offset > data.size || comp_size > data.size - offset).
  2. Partial write in handle_upload_pack(): write_error detection,
     upload abort instead of ACK with corrupt pack.
  3. goto pass_end on restore delta error: frees chunk_buf, dec_buf,
     uncomp_buf + unlink(full) to avoid empty partial files.
  4. brs_buffer_free(&comp_buf) in join_and_fail: prevents compression
     buffer leak on pipeline errors.
  5. tls:// and sftp:// rejected in VFS dispatch: clear error fprintf
     + return NULL, without creating junk local directories.
  6. VFS cleanup uses brs_uri_is_remote(): detects ssh/tls/sftp.
  7. VFS open uses brs_uri_is_remote(): consistent with cleanup.
  8. #include "brs_uri.h" in brs_repo_create.c: resolves dependency.
  9. #include <stdio.h> in brs_vfs_dispatch.c: resolves fprintf.
  10. #error guard in brs_repo_lock.h: aborts compilation if included
      alongside brs_lock.h (duplicate BrsRepoLock with different layout).
+ SNAPSHOT NAMING WITH MILLISECONDS: format YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM-SS-mmm_hash8.snap
  eliminates alphabetical collisions when two snapshots are created in the same
  second. Random hash no longer determines order.
+ parse_snapshot_timestamp CORRECTED: looked for ' ' (space) instead of '_'
  (underscore). Now supports format with ms (23 chars) and without ms (19 chars).
+ keep_by_period RESTORED: period retention function missing after
  previous patches. Necessary for prune --keep-daily/weekly/monthly/yearly.
+ Test suite expanded to 866: adds sections 93-103 (70 tests)
  functionally validating each of the 10 fixes.
+ Binary: ~1.2 MB (no size changes).
+ 866/866 regression tests passing.
+ NEW SECTION 28 in documentation: Security Audit and Fixes.

v2.3.2 (August 2026) — Remote Agent and Compilation Robustness Fixes

  handle_sync_index using strtoull with errno validation (instead of
  sscanf), preventing silent corruption when segment IDs exceed
  LLONG_MAX. If parsing fails, ENAMETOOLONG is sent to the client

  * baresnap-remote.c (handle_sync_index, idx_dir and full_path).

  * brs_tui.c (repo_path in do_backup).
  * brs_repo_create.c (local_path in ssh_load_all_indexes).
  * main.c (remote_bin_path, remote_cksum_cmd, remote_wc_cmd).
+ FIX IMPLICIT DECLARATION: Added #include "brs_util.h" in brs_vfs_ssh.c

+ FIX UNUSED PARAMETER: Real implementation of brs_vfs_ssh_fsync using
  ssh_lazy_fsync() and ssh_flush_write(), eliminating unused parameter 'f'
  warning and unused function 'ssh_lazy_fsync'.
+ FIX SIGN-COMPARE: Explicit cast to uint32_t in comparisons of
  brs_remote_rpc and brs_remote_unlink, eliminating sign-compare warnings.
+ DETERMINISTIC COMPILATION FIX: Change in compile.sh to use

v2.3.1 (August 2026) — Entropy Optimization, Dynamic Compression,
and 5 Critical Security/Stability Patches
+ ENTROPY FIREWALL (Early Abort): Injection of the inline function
  brs_is_chunk_incompressible() in the hot path to skip heavy
  compressors on white noise.
+ DYNAMIC COMPRESSION PER SNAPSHOT: Native coexistence of LZ4 and
  ZSTD chunks in the same physical pool.
+ DEFENSIVE VFS SANITIZATION: Automatic purging of zombie SSH virtual
  network contexts.
+ CRITICAL PATCHES (5 surgical fixes):
  1. brs_remote_v3.h: V3 opcodes migrated from 0x20-0x3F to 0x40-0x5F
     to eliminate collision with Smart SSH Protocol v2 (0x20-0x30).
     Responses migrated from 0xA0-0xBF to 0xC0-0xDF.
  2. brs_index.c (index_map_grow): Secondary load factor removed.
     Re-insertion during grow is now inline (without calling
     brs_index_map_put), preventing recursive OOM when hash table
     expands with millions of entries.
  3. brs_pack.c (brs_pack_writer_open_for_append): Guaranteed that
     CRC32C accurately absorbs dynamically appended bytes.
     crc_state is updated with brs_crc32c_update() over the range
     [data_start, data_end) before continuing the writer.
  4. brs_repo_common.c (pack_cache_free_entry): Implemented global
     counter tl_pack_cache_bytes for total LRU eviction per thread.
     Strict cleaning of free slots with memset() + data=NULL to
     disarm dangling pointers in RAM.
  5. brs_repo_health.c (brs_health_check): Hardened against false
     positives. If repo is encrypted but key is invalid, aborts with
     clean error instead of giving a false positive of corruption
     and moving healthy snapshots to damaged/ during --repair.
+ Remote agent FIX (baresnap-remote.c): Return value validation of
  snprintf in handle_list(). If combined path exceeds buffer,
  ENAMETOOLONG is sent to client and operation is aborted. Prevents
  silently corrupted backups due to long paths.
+ Test 79 FIX (Battery Death): Replaced fixed sleep with polling loop
  (100 iterations × 50ms) to ensure kill -9 applies while process is
  alive. Data expanded to 128 MB + 200 small files.
+ Test suite expanded to 796: adds sections 84-92 (health without
  false positives, index grow, CRC32C append, LRU eviction, and
  LIST/readdir suite with snprintf validation in remote agent).
+ Handle_list smoke test: 31/31 validation tests for snprintf patch.
